# DevFlow - the product, completely

This is the one document that explains the whole product: what it is for, how it is built, how it works end to end, how
it is run and how it is measured. Someone who reads only this file should understand the product and its
implementation. Where a detail matters, the module that owns it is named so it can be checked. It describes the code as
it is now; history lives in `docs/archive/` (see [ARCHIVE.md](ARCHIVE.md)). What is live and running now is in
[STATE.md](STATE.md); what is still to build is in [improvement-plan.md](improvement-plan.md); how to run, test and
deploy is in [HANDOVER.md](HANDOVER.md).

**In one paragraph.** DevFlow is a multi-agent software-development harness. A person gives it a product brief. A
simulated software company of language-model agents - product manager, architect, reviewers, QA, scrum master, technical
lead, specialist developers, security reviewer - turns the brief into a working, tested product in its own git
workspace, sprint by sprint. An acceptance suite written from the brief is locked before any code - for a product
planned as more than one sprint, each sprint's tests at that sprint's start - so "done" is defined by tests the
builders cannot change; the harness then runs, probes and fixes the product until the suite passes, and
hands over the code with a record of what was proven and what was not. It runs on a local model server the owner
provides and in Docker; a run takes one to three hours for a small product.

**Contents**

| Part | Sections |
|---|---|
| Purpose and pipeline | 1 Vision and purpose; 2 The pipeline end to end |
| The agents and what they work on | 3 Agents and roles; 4 State, documents and the workspace; 5 Stack packs and the language table |
| Proving and fixing | 6 Acceptance suite and verification; 7 Fix machinery; 8 Tools; 9 Switches and measurement |
| The system | 10 Architecture and deployment; 11 Server and API; 12 The web UI; 13 Data, records and observability |
| Quality and operation | 14 Quality gates and how the product is measured; 15 Security and safety; 16 Backing services and storage; 17 Operating limits and known gaps; 18 Repository map |

## 1. Vision and purpose

### 1.1 What DevFlow is

DevFlow is a multi-agent software-development harness. A person writes a product brief (Markdown, plain text, DOCX, PDF or CSV; optionally with an uploaded codebase or git repository to build on). The harness runs a simulated software company of LLM agents - product manager, architect, reviewers, QA, scrum master, technical lead, specialist developers, security reviewer - and returns a working, tested product in its own git workspace, with its unit tests, a locked acceptance suite, generated documents, a decision log, sprint records, CI and (for services) container files.

The harness is a LangGraph `StateGraph` (`app/graph/workflow.py`, nodes in `app/graph/nodes/`, `acceptance_nodes.py`, `engineering.py`, `escalation.py`, `delivery.py`). Each role is a Deep Agent built by one factory (`app/agents/role_agent.py`). Everything the harness can decide without a model - running tests, parsing files, probing a started product, reverting a bad fix - it decides mechanically. Models write and judge; the harness measures.

### 1.2 Who it is for

| Audience | What they get |
|---|---|
| An operator or product owner | Give a brief, pick a delivery mode and review points, receive a product plus the evidence of why it is done. Auto mode needs no human input. |
| A team taking over the product | A handover pack: the product archive with `HANDOVER.md`, process documents under `docs/process/`, the decision log, the acceptance suite the product was held to (`app/sprint/handover.py`, `app/sprint/product_archive.py`). |
| The harness's maintainers | A measured loop: benchmark briefs with hidden tests, an audit of every run, general fixes, switches measured before they are kept (section 9). |

It is built for self-hosted local models on one GPU first (a 35B mixture-of-experts class model is the measured baseline) and also runs on any OpenAI-compatible server, Anthropic or Gemini. Model plumbing is Part 2.

### 1.3 What "done" means

A run is done when its **locked acceptance suite passes**, not when an agent says so.

1. The criteria `AC-1..n` are read from the brief's own words (`app/acceptance/criteria.py`): its acceptance-criteria section, its features, its interface section line by line. The intake's success measures are the fallback only when the brief states none. Its other requirement lines and its constraints are read for what the product must have (`app/acceptance/brief_requirements.py`, PQ.1, LF.1330, always on, no model): an interface a person uses, database migrations, a container definition, API documentation, a CI pipeline. A kind no criterion carries yet becomes one criterion after the brief's own, in the brief's words; a kind already carried, a line ruling it out, and a choice of how the product is built (stack, storage, limits) add nothing. A behaviour a line states after its choice of how ("Authentication: JWT tokens with refresh mechanism": the words after "with", "including" or "supporting" naming a refresh, a rotation, an expiry or revocation, pagination, a request limit, retries, caching, an undo, a history or audit trail, notifications or reminders, or a "mechanism") that no criterion names becomes a criterion too, after the kinds (PQ.1 rest, LF.1559); a technology named there ("Java, with Spring Boot") stays a constraint. The first numbering records a decision naming each such line: added as AC-n, carried by AC-m, or a constraint. An added criterion the release cannot prove ships as a named scope cut, like any other.
2. QA writes at least one test per criterion (test names carry `[AC-n]`), the harness checks the suite mechanically, a QA lead reviews it, and it is **locked** (checksums plus a snapshot in `.harness/`). Writers cannot change it; only QA, reopened through a recorded decision, can.
3. The harness runs the suite itself and reads the runner's JUnit XML. "Which criteria pass" is the runner's record, never a model's summary.
4. Release requires every non-deferred criterion to pass, the product's own unit tests to pass (or be recorded), a handoff (README with run instructions, an API summary for services), and no secret in the code.

Outside the harness the product is scored by **hidden tests** written by a person from the brief and never shown to any run (`benchmarks/<family>/hidden/`), plus a **quality set** that holds the brief to its edges (`benchmarks/<family>/quality/`: malformed input, concurrent writes, ids that do not exist) and a **handoff score** (README, start script reads `PORT`, CI file, no thinking-aloud comments) from `scripts/bench/score.py`. A finished run whose product fails hidden tests is a **false acceptance**; the false-acceptance rate, not the mean score, says whether a finished run can be handed over unread.

A release is never silently "completed". `completion_gaps` lists what the release knew it did not prove; `app/completion_verdict.py` splits the gaps into ones that mean the product may not work (blocking), scope cuts (a criterion shipped deferred) and notes. Any gap kind not listed as a note or cut blocks.

### 1.4 What is good today, what is experimental

`app/stacks/support_matrix.py` is the one place that states support, from scored runs (hidden tests) on a local 35B-class model: a pack is **supported** with at least 5 scored runs and a mean hidden score of at least 0.90; every other pack is **experimental** (it has a pack, its commands are verified, evidence is too thin). A model whose measured tier is "weak" makes every stack experimental.

| Status | Packs (evidence in `support_matrix.EVIDENCE`) |
|---|---|
| Supported | `python-http-api`, `python-cli`, `python-library`, `fullstack-page-fastapi`, `node-http-api`, `node-hono-native-api`, `browser-javascript`, `browser-react`, `fullstack-node` |
| Experimental | every other pack, including the Go, Rust, Java, C#, PHP and Ruby service packs (fewer than 5 scored runs or a lower mean; Rust/Axum is the weakest on the table) and the Vue, Svelte, Angular, Nest, Django, Next and TypeScript/Go/Rust CLI and library packs |
| No pack | any stack no pack covers is **custom**: an agent sets it up and the harness proves the setup (`app/stacks/custom_setup.py`); the release has no packaged CI or container files and says so |

Experimental also covers the harness's optional switches that are still being measured (section 9), hosted-model tuning and the "premium" bundles. The table in `support_matrix.py` is edited by hand when new scored runs move a stack; `support_matrix.for_the_brief` warns before a run starts when the brief names an experimental language.

### 1.5 Principles

| Principle | Where it is enforced |
|---|---|
| **Tests lock the definition of done.** The suite is written from the brief before anything is built, reviewed, locked, and run by the harness. | `app/acceptance/suite.py`, `app/graph/acceptance_nodes.py`, write guards in `app/tools/repository.py` |
| **Every fix is general.** A fix to the harness is technology-, framework- and product-agnostic, carries a test proven to fail on the old code, and names no run. | repository rules (`AGENTS.md`), unit tests |
| **Stack-specific content lives in three places only:** a stack pack (`app/stacks/packs/`), `app/plugins/seed.py`, and per-language tables (`app/stacks/languages.py`, `app/tools/sandbox.py` tables). Core code and shared prompts know no language. | section 5 |
| **Measure before keeping.** A change whose value is in doubt ships behind a switch, off or measured with two runs per family on and off on one harness commit, and is kept only if no hidden or quality score falls and its target KPI moves. | `app/switches.py`, `docs/measuring.md` (section 9) |
| **Bug fixes and quality changes are always on.** Only optional, non-quality improvements keep a switch. | `app/switches.BUILT_IN` |
| **Harness decides, models propose.** Documents are rendered by templates from structured answers; counts, gates and caps are code. | `app/doc_templates/`, `app/graph/` |
| **One writer of each thing.** The suite is QA's, harness files are the harness's, a work order's files are its specialist's. | section 3.4 |
| **Fail loudly, degrade gracefully.** Every terminal state but a clean release writes a marker into the workspace; a check that cannot run says so instead of failing the product. | `app/graph/terminal_state.py`, `app/observability/degraded.py`, `app/acceptance/unmeasured.py` |

---

## 2. The pipeline end to end

### 2.1 How routing works

The graph has one conditional-edge router. Every node returns a `stage` value naming the next node (or a pause value); `_route_from_stage` (`workflow.py`) sends control there. The set of legal targets is `_NODE_NAMES`; a stage name that is not a node is answered `failed` by `_owning_node`, never a `KeyError`. A node can therefore hand to any other node (escalation, change request, back to QA), which is how every loop below is expressed.

- **Entry router** (`_entry_router`): a fresh run enters at `product_manager`; a resumed run enters the node its checkpointed `stage` names. Iterated inner stages (`specialist_backend_0`, `test_round_2`, `defect_fix_1_0`, `acceptance_author_1`) are mapped back to the node that owns the loop by `_ITERATED_STAGE_OWNERS`.
- **Two kinds of pause**: `awaiting_approval:<stage>` (a role agent's gated tool call, such as a write, needs a human) and `awaiting_user_review:<point>` (an optional review gate). A skip is its own status and never looks like an approval; an optional review can never bypass the mandatory architecture gate.
- **Parallel fan-out** (`execution_mode == "parallel"` in settings): the router returns `Send(...)` objects for `specialist_worker` (work orders), `defect_fix_worker` (a batch of defects) and `review_worker` (reviews). The gather nodes are the only writers of `stage` after a fan-out. Width is `concurrent_requests()` = `HARNESS_MAX_PARALLEL` (default 6) bounded by the model server's slots. **Linear** mode runs one at a time. Work orders that share a file are staggered (`_that_do_not_share_a_file`).
- **Per-node wrapper**: every stage node snapshots the workspace under its stage name before it runs (`_snapshotting`, refs under `refs/harness/stages/`), which is what makes rewind possible (section 4.6). Agent nodes carry a LangGraph `RetryPolicy` (3 attempts, 2 s backoff doubling to 30 s) that does not retry a provider out of quota.
- **Review points** (`review_policy`, opt-in per run): `product_intake`, `technical_specification`, `acceptance_tests`, `sprint`, `release`, `escalation`. With none on, the harness decides each gate and records a decision (`app/decisions.py`).

### 2.2 The graph

```
 product_manager ─▶ [user_review_product_intake]* ─▶ tools_models ─▶ stack_selector
                                                                        │
                                                                        ▼
                                              specialist_consultation (one advisor per entry)
                                                                        │
                                                                        ▼
                      ┌─────────────▶ principal_architect  ◀───── changes_required ─┐
                      │                    │                                         │
                      │                    ▼                                         │
                      │             architecture_review ─────────────────────────────┘
                      │                    │ approved (or findings that do not block)
                      │                    ▼
                      │          [user_review_specification]*
                      │                    ▼
   escalation(design) ┘  (revisions spent)  scaffold  ── unproven twice ──▶ escalation
                                           │
                                           ▼
                                   acceptance_author ◀──┐ send-backs (≤3 rounds, harness checks + QA lead)
                                           ▼            │
                                   acceptance_review ───┘
                                           │ LOCK the suite
                                           ▼
                                  [user_review_acceptance]*
                                           ▼
                                      scrum_master  (sprints = vertical slices, each owns AC-n)
                                           ▼
   ┌───────────────────────────── per sprint ──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │ technical_lead ──(criteria already pass)──▶ close the sprint with nothing to build     │
   │   │  (they pass, a round's probes find a fault) ──▶ test_and_triage, no work orders    │
   │   │  (dispute due after a failed attempt) ──▶ acceptance_dispute ─▶ back to the lead   │
   │   ▼                                                                                    │
   │ specialist_dispatch ─▶ specialist_worker ×N ─▶ specialist_gather                       │
   │   ▼   (fast delivery: files drafted from the specification first)                      │
   │   ▼   (thorough: an order not done goes back to its builder once, LF.1299)             │
   │ ci_gate ─▶ code_review ─▶ test_and_triage ◀──────────────┐                             │
   │                               │ defects                  │ next test round             │
   │                               ▼                          │                             │
   │                       defect_remediation ─▶ (fix guard, revert, best checkpoint) ──────┘│
   │                               │ attempts spent ──▶ re-plan (technical_lead)             │
   │                               │                    ≥ limit ──▶ escalation               │
   │                               ▼ all clear                                              │
   │ security_review ─▶ advice_review ─▶ milestone_review                                   │
   │       (or review_worker ×N ─▶ review_gather)     │ revise ─▶ technical_lead (≤3)       │
   │                                                  ▼ accept                              │
   │                         [user_review_sprint]* ─▶ sprint_advance ─▶ next sprint         │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                           ▼ last sprint accepted
                                        release ──── held (≤2) ──▶ technical_lead
                                           ▼
                                [user_review_release]* ─▶ complete
   escalation ─▶ (auto) defer criteria and go on / build from latest design / failed
              ─▶ (asked) user_review_escalation ─▶ escalation_accept | escalation_retry
   change_request (a reviewer's change, CR-n) ─▶ acceptance_author ─▶ lock ─▶ technical_lead
   * = pauses only when that review point is switched on for the run
```

### 2.3 Models by stage (summary)

`GraphContext` (`app/graph/context.py`) carries four models: `model` (default), `planning_model`, `judgement_model`, `building_model`. With no judgement model configured, all four are the default. Planning stages (intake, tools, stack, sprint plan, work orders) run on `planning_model`, which is the judgement model when `PLAN_ON_JUDGEMENT_MODEL` is on (default) and delivery is thorough. Judging stages (architect, architecture review, QA lead, test rounds, fixes, code, security and milestone reviews, dispute) run on `judgement_model`. Building stages use `ctx.the_build_model(...)`: the default model (reasoning off in fast delivery), escalating to the judgement model after `ESCALATE_AFTER_ATTEMPTS` (default 2) failed fixes or restarts, after a reverted fix, or on any retried sprint (`the_model_for_this_attempt`: a re-plan, two milestone revisions, a held release or a sprint escalation; design escalations do not count). The worker builds a run's graph with no model, and a run on the build model only has no judgement model, so in a local run all four are None and each stage builds the default model itself; a helper that calls a model directly - the recurring-cause diagnosis, the candidate fixes, the screenshot rubric - takes `context.a_model_to_call(ctx, preferred)`: the fix's own model, else the judgement model, else the run's, else the deployment's default model built as a stage builds it (LF.1430: none of the three had ever run on a local model). Details: section 3.3.

### 2.4 Stage reference

For each stage: owner module, what it reads, what it writes, how it fails and where control goes. "Failed" means the `failed` node (`closing.failed_node`), which always records a reason and writes the workspace's terminal marker.

| # | Stage (node) | Owner | Purpose | Inputs | Outputs | Failure and next |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `product_manager` | `nodes/planning.py`, `agents/product_manager.py` | Turn the brief into a structured intake | brief text, repository context | `product_intake` (requirements, success measures, constraints, workflows, open questions); `docs/product_intake.md`; project directory pinned (`.project-directory`) | No structured answer: restart on a fresh conversation (`WORKFLOW_MAX_STRUCTURED_RETRIES`, default 2), then failed. The harness then puts back what the brief states and the model dropped (`with_the_constraints_the_brief_states`, `with_the_measures_the_brief_states`, `with_the_workflows_its_measures_require`, deferrals touching required scope). With `PRODUCT_RESEARCH` on and the run owner's consent, its request first carries cited suggestions from comparable products, and any it put outside deferred scope is moved there before those guards (`agents/product_research.py`, 7.6). Next: `tools_models`, or the intake gate when its review is on or `INTAKE_ASKS_FIRST` finds open questions a person answers. |
| 2 | `tools_models` | `nodes/planning.py`, `agents/tools_models.py` | Evidence-based comparison of frameworks, storage, providers | intake | `docs/tools_and_models.md`; the sandbox pins every language the stack names (`pin_project_stack`) | Its answer offers only the comparison sections the intake's kind of product needs (PA.14, LF.1384, always on; `tools_models.sections_for`): frameworks, storage and testing tools for every kind, deployment for a page or a service; model providers, queues, authentication and observability only when the intake's own words ask for them; every section when no kind can be told. A section it was not offered is left empty. A missing intake sends control back to `product_manager` (bounded), then failed. Next: `stack_selector`. |
| 3 | `stack_selector` | `nodes/planning.py`, `agents/stack_selector.py`, `app/stacks/__init__.py` | One technology stack and the stack pack that fits | intake, tools, the brief's own constraint lines, the list of supported stacks | `stack_selection` (language, framework, testing framework, product kind), `stack_pack` (or None), `docs/technology_stack.md`. A page product that names no browser runner is corrected to one (`the_browser_runner_this_stack_forgot`). | Next: `specialist_consultation`. No pack fits: the stack is custom (section 5.7). |
| 4 | `specialist_consultation` | `nodes/planning.py`, `agents/specialist_advice.py` | One structured answer per needed specialist before the specification: UI/UX when there is an interface, database when it keeps data, backend when it serves requests, security and reliability when it serves requests or handles sign-in, personal data, money or files, testing when there are criteria | brief, intake, stack | `specialist_advice[role]`, items numbered `UX-n`, `DB-n`, `BE-n`, `SR-n`, `TS-n` | One advisor per visit, looping on itself until all are asked. Behind `DOMAIN_CRAFT_CHECKLISTS` (SK.2, off): the database and backend advisers are also asked for the data and service bars (constraints within the brief, indexes, created and changed times never returned unasked, migrations when asked; one error shape, the status code per failure, authorisation on owned records, no input ignored), and the UI/UX adviser and the architect are told the design tokens the run's pack ships (read from the pack's stylesheet) and that the interface design names them, never raw values (PQ.6, PQ.9). Behind `ADVICE_SIZED_TO_THE_BRIEF` (PA.9, LF.1385, off until measured; `agents/advice_sized.py`): every adviser but testing gives at most 3 items for a brief of up to 10 acceptance criteria and 5 up to 20 (10 beyond), each opening `[asked] "<the brief's words>"` or `[beyond]`, the asked ones kept first when an answer is cut; the architect and the part writers are told an item beyond the brief is declined unless its decision line quotes the brief line that needs it, and the harness records one adopted without such a quote as declined. Next: `principal_architect`. |
| 5 | `principal_architect` | `nodes/specification.py`, `agents/principal_architect.py`, `graph/specification_parts.py`, `agents/research_brief.py` | Technical specification and ADRs; adopts or declines every advice item; writes `interface_design` for products with an interface and an `interface_contract` (one strict line per route, control or command, each naming its criterion) | intake, tools, stack, advice, scaffold layout, naming standard, the harness's pre-read of registries and docs (`research_notes`; each page without its furniture - markup, badges, lone links, rules, sponsor and contributor sections - LF.1471, and behind `DOCUMENTATION_EXCERPTS_BOUNDED`, off until measured, `docs/measuring.md` M21, only its passages that use a word of the brief or the topic, at most 1,200 characters a library), prior review findings | `technical_specification`, `docs/technical_specification.md` (rendered), `architect_research` kept for retries | Sections left empty are re-asked (`MAX_SPECIFICATION_REPAIRS` = 2); layout and startup problems repaired in place. Behind `INTERFACE_STATES_AS_CRITERIA` (PQ.8, LF.1331, off until measured; `agents/interface_states.py`), a product with a page is asked for `Empty:`, `Busy:` and `Failed:` lines in `interface_design` (the view, an ARIA role, the words shown, outside any list), and a draft missing one goes back once for `interface_design` only. The built page is then held to them (PQ.8 proof, LF.1380, same switch): the page check loads the page again with the requests its scripts make held back and with them failing (`statesOf`), and a state the page shows in none of those trees - nor, when the page cannot be put in that state at load, anywhere in its code - or shows under another role, is one major `ui_ux` defect (`Interface states: ...`, `interface_states.not_shown`, raised by `analysis/interface_style_checks.states_defect`). In fast delivery the core is written first and the other sections at once in parts (`specification_parts`), and a revision rewrites only the sections the review touched. **The first draft is made consistent before its first review** (PA.7, LF.1383, always on; `graph/specification_consistency.py`): the values its sections repeat are compared mechanically - each route's status codes against `api_and_event_contracts` (every answer) and `interface_contract` (only a different success code), each source file named with its folder against the file plan (tests and files the harness keeps aside) and a name the plan gives another extension, the file each start command runs, each limit given for one subject, and each advice item's decision (decided twice, or declined and then cited or specified elsewhere); statements that contradict each other in different words, which no value comparison finds ("a malformed data file makes it exit" against "a corrupted file starts it empty"), are named first by one call shown the draft's statements numbered, for a draft of at least 8,000 characters (PA.7 rest, LF.1561, `graph/specification_meaning.py`); only when some disagree, one call is shown just those lines and rewrites the ones that must change, every other line kept. A revision is not passed through it. Next: `architecture_review`. |
| 6 | `architecture_review` | `nodes/specification.py`, `agents/architecture_reviewer.py` | Independent review for completeness, feasibility, security, unjustified assumptions | specification, brief, environment | `architecture_review` (status, findings with severity); `docs/architecture_review.md`; a decision record | After the model's verdict the harness adds its own findings (success measure dropped, a brief feature deferred, contract lines nothing can read, the same command in another order, behaviour given to kept files, a kept signature called otherwise) and runs `_a_pass_over_its_own_findings`. Approved, or only non-gating findings: to `scaffold` (or the specification gate). Gating findings: `architecture_revision_count` += 1, back to `principal_architect`. At `MAX_ARCHITECTURE_REVISIONS` (6; `WORKFLOW_MAX_ARCHITECTURE_REVISIONS`; 2 in fast delivery) the run escalates with `kind="design"` (2.8). **Thorough delivery gates development on the gating findings** (LF.1318, owner 2026-11-25, always on; `graph/design_gate.py`): every revision is reviewed again in full (`ONE_DESIGN_REVIEW` applies to fast delivery only; `HARNESS_FINDINGS_RECHECKED` only while no reviewer-raised gating finding is open); the re-review gets the earlier findings numbered and answers each blocking/major one in `earlier_findings` (number, resolved, where); a "resolved" counts only when `where` quotes the specification (6 consecutive words of it, `design_gate.quotes_the_specification`), one resting on how the harness runs the acceptance tests (the stack's acceptance runner with its configuration, workers or processes) is refused and sent back, and one naming a tool of the chosen stack is resolved *on a claim to verify*: the decision says so, and `.harness/design-claims.json` is given to the lead and every builder under "Design claims to verify, not facts" (with the specification rulings; LF.1440, always on); a "resolved" that quotes nothing usable is checked by the harness itself first (LF.1504, always on, `graph/design_gate_check.py`): where the finding says a section is missing ("there is no `error_catalogue` key", "the X section ... does not exist", "the X section is '_Not specified._'") or a backticked name or route is missing that the specification it was raised on did not name (kept on the ledger entry as `absent`), and the current specification (its fields, or the rendered document's headings) now has it, the finding is resolved on the harness's own quote - the section's first line, or the line naming the term - recorded as "resolved: checked by the harness - <quote>", the tool and suite rules above applied to that quote; a section named only as a reference or as where something is, a section that is the subject of another verb, and a finding that says something is stated wrongly (wrong, contradicts, conflicts, malformed, ...) still need the reviewer's quote; the same check runs on every open reviewer-raised finding before the escalation waits for a person (`design_gate.holds_after_checking`), except one the last re-review said is still open or raised again; the workflow's own checks are asked again on every review (once-per-run flags and the last-revision guard do not apply); a ledger in state (`design_gate`) holds each gating finding (G1, G2 ...) until the re-review says it is resolved or no longer raises it (a check: no longer finds it) or a person approves it - one the reviewer says is still open but does not list again is put back into the review, so the next revision is told of it; each end is a decision record ("resolved in review round N" or "approved by the person at the escalation gate"). When the harness approves without a second review (`HARNESS_FINDINGS_RECHECKED`, `ONE_DESIGN_REVIEW`), the approval is built from the last review in state: its `approval_conditions`, `accepted_risks` and `earlier_findings` are kept, the findings emptied, and the decision says how many conditions and risks are carried (LF.1511, always on; `agents/design_review_conditions.py`). The approved review's conditions go, verbatim and within about 1,500 characters, under "Conditions the design review approved the specification on" to `scrum_master`, `technical_lead` and every work order's builder (linear and parallel), and to the judges of the built work (the code, security and milestone reviews), so a judge reads what the builders were given; its accepted risks to the two planners only, as known risks (LF.1512, always on). |
| 7 | `scaffold` | `acceptance_nodes.scaffold`, `app/stacks/scaffold.py` | Set the project up so its tests run before any agent writes code | `stack_pack`, product name, brief | `.harness/stack-pack.json` (the contract), the pack's files, installed dependencies, `scaffold_result`; smoke tests proven then removed | Whole scaffold retried once; unproven twice: escalation with the problem ("nothing is built on an unproven setup"). A seeded codebase with its own manifest is built on as it is. No pack: the custom setup stage (5.7). Next: `acceptance_author` (which numbers the criteria and, for a product that may be planned as more than one sprint, hands straight to `scrum_master`: §6.3, LF.1266). |
| 8 | `acceptance_author` | `acceptance_nodes.acceptance_author`, `graph/acceptance_batches.py` | QA writes the suite from the numbered criteria and the interface contract | criteria, stack, API reference from the specification, brief, feedback of the last review | test files under the pack's acceptance directory; criteria QA says cannot be automated are deferred with a reason unless a test covers them | May write in batches of criteria for a model that finds the whole suite too much (`BATCH_ACCEPTANCE_AUTHOR`). **Past the author's room it is written a file at a time** (PA.4, LF.1571, always on, `graph/suite_by_file.py`): a first writing (a whole suite or one sprint's addition) whose request, memory as the stage opens, the writer's measured framing (50,000 characters) and 3,000 characters of work a criterion fill its summary trigger is written in parts of one criterion group each (the model's batch size, else 4), each its own conversation writing one new test file, holding only the interface contract's lines its criteria name (and those naming none) and the suite's shared files (those declaring no test) whole; a part whose criteria are covered is not asked again, and one left without a test is asked for once more alone. One `suite_by_file` transcript record; `loop.sh effects` counts them. A suite that fits, and a repair, are one request. Written a sprint at a time (§6.3), it writes only the criteria of the addition open now, in new files beside the locked ones. Past the memory's room, from its first summary its memory holds its own files numbered as they are now (LF.1288). A later sprint's writers are handed the product as the earlier sprints built it - the lines of its code that declare or mount a route with their handlers' signatures, the shapes those handlers name, the signatures of the earlier locked tests' helpers (`acceptance/product_interface.py`, bounded) - and their memory leaves the product's code out, named in one line (LF.1362). No structured answer after its restarts (any `acceptance_author_*` stage: a first writing, a sprint's addition, a batch, a repair sent back to it): the harness builds its answer from the suite's files on disk - `changed_files` the suite files new or changed since the last lock (`suite.changed_since_the_lock`), one blocker saying it gave no answer and where its last attempt ended (never read as a criterion that cannot be automated) - writes nothing, and the review, dry run and lock rules judge the files; with no suite file at all the run fails as before (LF.1365). Next: `acceptance_review`. |
| 9 | `acceptance_review` | `acceptance_nodes.acceptance_review`, `agents/acceptance_reviewer.py` | Mechanical checks, then a QA lead judges whether each test would fail if its criterion were unmet; then lock The QA lead reads the suite, the criteria and the documents, never the product's own code: a read of an application file is refused by name (`agents/reads_of_the_product.py`, LF.1531). Where the suite is written a sprint at a time, "What running it showed" names each failed test of a criterion the sprint builds once its tests are locked "expected now: AC-n is built by this sprint", under a line saying such failures are no evidence against the tests; a failure of a criterion an earlier sprint built, and every failure at the release, stays as it is (`by_sprint.what_running_it_showed`, LF.1532, always on). | the suite, one run of it against the scaffold, brief, specification; past its room, the suite's files held numbered in its memory and named in the request in place of their text (LF.1286) | `docs/acceptance_tests.md`; `.harness/acceptance-snapshot/`, `.harness/acceptance-locks/vN/` with diffs; the contract recorded at lock; a decision record | Back to `acceptance_author` while problems remain and `acceptance_round < MAX_ACCEPTANCE_ROUNDS` (3; the QA lead reads the suite once even at the limit; fast delivery sends back once). A suite that cannot run or proves nothing is never locked for `rounds < MAX+2` and then fails the run. At the limit, tests too weak, partial, or that no product could pass go back once more (`ONCE_AT_THE_LIMIT`), then lock with the problems open (recorded as gaps). In both delivery modes, a QA-lead finding at the limit that a test fails on a correct product (graded blocking, or gating in those words) sends those tests back once for repair of those tests alone before any lock (`suite_gate.repair_before_the_lock`, LF.1442, always on). At the first writing, before anything is built, the tests that already pass are kept by name (`acceptance/passed_unbuilt.py`, LF.1446): a criterion all of whose tests are among them is shown "passes, but only on tests that passed before anything was built" in the lead's criteria and the time budget's reason, and the foundation rule does not count those tests as the sprint's passes. **Thorough delivery locks no suite with a gating finding open** (TG.2, LF.1370, always on; `graph/suite_gate.py`): each QA-lead finding carries a severity (blocking/major/minor/suggestion, any vocabulary read by `agents/severity.py`; a bare line is `minor`, which locks as before), each harness finding the severity of what it breaks (`suite_gate.severity_of_a_check`: blocking for a test no product can pass - every `tests_that_cannot_pass` check, a body read from a 204, a success status the contract does not give; major for a test that passes a product not meeting its criterion - `tests_guarded_by_the_answer` - or a criterion with no test; minor for the rest: how controls are found, how closely outputs are held, contract lines no test calls, tests that pass before anything is built, files the harness put back). At the lock of a suite written before its development (not one reopened for a repair), a blocking or major finding sends the run to `escalation` with `kind="suite"`, which waits for a person whatever the review policy; a ledger (`suite_gate`: S1, S2 ...) holds each until a later review no longer raises it ("resolved in acceptance round N") or a person approves it; while one of the QA lead's is open, the QA lead reads the suite again. Fast delivery and a reopened suite lock as before. Next: `scrum_master` (or the acceptance gate); written a sprint at a time, the sprint's `technical_lead`. Section 6. |

### 2.5 The test round in detail

`test_and_triage` runs after every implementation batch and every fix round. `test_round` gives each round its own inner thread so a test engineer never re-reads a stale conversation.

1. Clear the previous round's throwaway files (scratch names, test-named probes no suite runs, and a fix or testing stage's probe known by what it is - a code file it created, or a command of it created, in the project's or the workspace's top folder, outside the work orders' file table, the specification and the sprint plan, not a file a tool loads by its name alone (configuration, `__init__`, `index`/`main`), named by no other file and reached by nothing in the code graph, `analysis/stage_scratch.py`, LF.1533; the same rule keeps such a file out of the cross-file checks, the fix-file choice and the reviews' held files, and the write that creates one tells the stage once to name a probe `tmp-*` or delete it; never a test file inside a test folder of its own, LF.1262); put back a deliberate mutation left by a dead worker.
2. `acceptance_nodes.acceptance_for_round` runs the locked suite (once per round, on first entry; a measurement of an unchanged product is reused, `MEASURE_ONCE`). Locked tests that **cannot pass** whatever is built are not product defects: the suite is reopened for QA first (`reopen_the_suite_for`), and once repairs are spent those tests are deferred and named (`beyond_repair`).
3. Who runs the round: the **harness** itself when the evidence is clean (a locked, ran suite passing everything due, unit tests passing, nothing else proven wrong) or always in fast delivery; otherwise the **testing specialist** on the judgement model. No model reads a green round. With `MEASURED_ROUND_WHEN_RED` on (group "Efficiency", off until measured, `docs/measuring.md` M-LF.1395, `graph/measured_round.py`), a thorough round whose locked suite ran and fails due criteria is the harness's own too, as in fast delivery (transcript record `measured_round`); the trade is that what only the specialist finds beyond the suite waits for a round whose suite is green. The split, always on (LF.1394): the harness measures, the round reports, the fixer diagnoses. A specialist's round whose due criteria fail is told, after the suite's result, that the harness raises each of them as a defect itself after the round (`failing_due_defects`, with the tests' messages and the server's log) and sends a fixer with it to find the cause; that it is not to report them again, work out why they fail, run the suite again or write a script to reproduce one; and that its report is what the measurement does not show (the unit tests' failures, an error printed while tests passed, a rule held on one path only, a fault in a test of its own). Each failed test in the suite's result and in a failing criterion's defect line is shown by what it said (`acceptance/failure_said.py`, LF.1393): the first line past the runner's own heading for the test (`file:line:col › title ────`) and the expected and received values printed after it, not the heading alone; for a page test also its `Locator:`, `Timeout:` and second `Error:` lines, or the first thing its call log waited for (LF.1459). The same heading-aware reader (`failure_said.first_line_said`, `is_a_heading`) gives what a test threw (`first_failing_step.what_it_threw`, so `judged_fixes` reads it too), the milestone review's failing lines, the last try at a deferred criterion and the test-rewrite grouping (LF.1461). The page a failed test met is cut to 1,500 characters around the first line naming what its locator sought, with the lines that hold it above and "N more line(s)" at each cut, and the eight pages go to each criterion's first failure before a second of one (`acceptance/failure_page.py`, LF.1460). After a specialist's round, whatever the delivery, a failing harness unit run's grouped causes are carried to the round's defects about those tests (their file or name in the defect's text), or, when none is about them and no unit-test or unit-regression defect of the harness's is open, the harness raises its own unit-test defect as fast delivery does (`graph/unit_causes_carried.py`, LF.1457).
4. The round adds the harness's own findings: the product does not start (one defect folding every start, compile, install and unit-compile finding, `graph/start_first.py`), the foundation smoke's one defect when a started product cannot make an account, sign in or make and read back a record (every other defect held while it is open, `acceptance/foundation_smoke.py`, LF.1268), contract server errors, probes on a green suite (concurrent writes, wrong-shape body, edge requests, page probe), the page check's defect, unit tests that fail beside a green suite (set aside or raised, `UNIT_TEST_QUARANTINE`), the scaffold's stub still served, a production build that fails. A unit run that exits as a pass having collected no test is a failing one once unit tests were ordered (unit test files written, or planned in the work orders), its output naming where the runner collects and each misplaced file's collected path (`stacks/unit_test_home.py`, LF.1263); the same module names a work order's unit test files where the runner collects them, and the write guard refuses a new test file anywhere else with that path.
5. A defect the round (or a code review) raised in its own words, stating no error, carries the error lines its stage's own command results printed that its words match - at least two of its distinctive words, up to three lines (`acceptance/errors_as_printed.py`, LF.1412); the ledger's `raised` record names it (`error_as_printed`). A dotted exception name with no message (`sqlalchemy.exc.InvalidRequestError.`) is an error line.
   Defects are de-duplicated (`_each_defect_once`, `_one_defect_per_cause`), one open defect per cause key, and a defect raised again refreshes the open one instead of queueing a second fixer.
6. `record_test_run` charges a round against `max_attempts` (3, `app/sprint/__init__.py`) only when it found a product defect; a round with only mutation findings open, or one whose measurement the harness could not take (`unmeasured`), charges nothing. A hard ceiling of `ROUNDS_PER_ATTEMPT_CEILING` (2) rounds per attempt in all bounds that exemption.
7. Open defects: `defect_remediation`. Nothing open: `is_sprint_acceptable` (regression coverage, accepted risks) then `security_review`. Thorough delivery, once a sprint attempt (LF.1299, `unfinished_build.at_a_round`): when half or more of the round's defects say the work is not built (a 404 on a contract route of the sprint's criteria that no code serves, a file a work order named that does not exist, a work order's file still a stub) and a work order covers them, they are closed as `superseded` and go to that order's builder (`specialist_dispatch`, told what the round found; ledger event `sent to its builder`), the round charges no attempt, and the other defects stay open for the fix round after the next test round.
8. When the fix round before closed nothing on an unchanged product and no fixer of it wrote a change (none reverted, repaired forward or left a file changed; LF.1445), the attempt ends there (`FIX_STAGES_THAT_CLOSE_NOTHING`).

### 2.6 Sprint, attempt and round vocabulary and caps

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Sprint** | One entry of `sprint_plan.sprints`; `current_sprint_index`. Owns criteria; its **due** criteria are its own plus every earlier sprint's (the last sprint answers for all). |
| **Attempt** | One planning of a sprint: the lead's plan, the build, and the test-fix rounds until the attempt ends. A re-plan starts a new attempt (`sprint_replan_count`). Every attempt is appended to `sprint_attempts` and never rewritten. |
| **Test round** | One `test_and_triage` pass (`test_round`). |
| **Fix round** | Every `defect_fix_*` stage between two test rounds. |
| **Milestone revision** | The milestone review sent the plan back to the lead (`milestone_revision_count`). Counted separately from re-plans. |

| Cap | Value | Owner | At the cap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Architecture revisions | 6 (`WORKFLOW_MAX_ARCHITECTURE_REVISIONS`); 2 in fast delivery | `workflow.MAX_ARCHITECTURE_REVISIONS`, `_the_revision_limit` | escalation `kind="design"`: fast delivery builds from the latest specification, open findings recorded as known risks (behind `FAST_DESIGN_GATE_ON_PRODUCT_BREAKING`, a product-breaking finding first gets a second revision and a review after each); thorough delivery with a gating finding open waits for a person (2.8) |
| Specification section repairs | 2 | `MAX_SPECIFICATION_REPAIRS` | proceeds to review |
| Acceptance review rounds | 3 (+2 for a suite that cannot run or proves nothing); fast delivery: 1 send-back | `acceptance_nodes.MAX_ACCEPTANCE_ROUNDS` | lock with problems open, or fail when the suite cannot run |
| Suite repairs (reopen after lock) | 2; 4 in all counting tests never sent before | `MAX_SUITE_REPAIRS`, `MAX_SUITE_REPAIRS_IN_ALL` | later failures of those tests are "beyond repair": deferred and named |
| Reclassifications by dispute | max(2, a tenth of the locked tests) | `MOST_RECLASSIFICATIONS` | further rulings are refused |
| Remediation attempts (charged rounds) | 3 | `sprint.DEFAULT_MAX_REMEDIATION_ATTEMPTS` | `_replan_or_escalate` |
| Re-plans of one sprint | 3 re-plans (four attempts); past it the sprint escalates | `MAX_MILESTONE_REVISIONS` used by `_replan_or_escalate` | escalation |
| Milestone revisions | 3 (`MAX_MILESTONE_REVISIONS`); fast: 2 then findings are recorded as risks | `nodes/milestone.py` | escalation (thorough) or "record the findings as risks and go on" (fast) |
| Escalations per run | 3 (a design escalation sent back by the person and a suite held for a person are not counted) | `escalation.MAX_ESCALATIONS` | the run fails |
| Release attempts | 2 | `delivery.MAX_RELEASE_ATTEMPTS` | escalation |
| Structured-answer restarts | 2 | `WORKFLOW_MAX_STRUCTURED_RETRIES` | the stage fails the run (or a stage-specific fallback) |
| Model calls per stage | 40 (`MODEL_CALLS_PER_STAGE`); defect-fix stage 16 (`FIX_STAGE_MODEL_CALLS`) | `agents/settled_answer.py` | tools withheld two calls before (a stage offered a tool that changes a file keeps its writers there, LF.1270, every writing stage since LF.1519), the answer is forced or salvaged; a tool refused the same way four times is withheld, the tool layer's own "Error invoking tool" refusal included (LF.1492); after three redundant reads refused or eight pointed at the memory, reads are decided file by file, not the read tool withheld whole (LF.1517) |
| Model calls per defect across attempts | 60 (`DEFECT_CALLS_AT_MOST`) | `graph/defect_budget.py` | the defect is left open with its attempts as the diagnosis |
| Inner agent supersteps | 160 (`INNER_AGENT_RECURSION_LIMIT` = 40 calls x 4.0); outer graph 100 | `workflow.py` | derived so the call budget binds first |
| Outer graph step limit | 100 supersteps an invoke (`DEFAULT_RECURSION_LIMIT`) | `server/step_limit.py`, `runner._run_loop` | the run goes on from its checkpoint in a fresh invoke (transcript `step_limit`); after 2 stops in a row with no progress (sprint, test or acceptance round, defect closed or worked on, new stage output) it is left paused for a person |
| Defects a round raises from one source | 10 (`MOST_FROM_ONE_SOURCE_PER_ROUND`) | `graph/nodes/triage.py` | the largest causes keep their own fixer; the rest are bundled into one defect of that source |
| Build-model failures before the judgement model | 2 (`ESCALATE_AFTER_ATTEMPTS`) | `models/roles.escalate_after` | the stage or defect moves up a tier |

**End of an attempt** is one decision from one measurement (`graph/end_of_attempt.decide`), in this order: put the **best attempt** back if the product is now worse than the best acceptance count seen (one test fewer is already worse; `KEEP_THE_BEST_ATTEMPT`); **settle** if the due criteria all pass and only the product's own unit tests or findings read from files are open (`SETTLE_WHEN_ACCEPTANCE_IS_GREEN`); **stop** if the last two attempts did not beat the best (`STALL_RULE`), if a near-green sprint was already planned again once (`NEAR_GREEN_REPLANNED_ONCE`) or if the sprint's time budget is past (`SPRINT_TIME_BUDGET`: 30 minutes plus 12 per criterion or planned work item, whichever is more, plus 4 per adopted specialist item the sprint plan places in the sprint); otherwise **re-plan**. A stop or settle takes the escalation path with the reason recorded. The budget's active minutes run from the sprint's first plan (from the escalation's re-plan for a sprint planned again for its foundation, LF.1448) and leave out silences over 30 minutes, the gaps that end on a `resumed` record, the review stages, and the minutes inside a streak of failed model calls (`model_error` records with no `model_response` between them: the server failing, not the sprint working). It is read inside an attempt too: once the sprint has run twice its budget, a test round sends no fix round and a fix round sends no more fixers - but only after the attempt's first fix round, so every attempt tries its defects once (`sprint_time_budget.a_fix_round_already_sent`, read from the fixers each defect records).

### 2.7 Release

`delivery.release` (cancel check first):

1. Every criterion is recorded due (`record_everything_due`), so the release runs the whole suite. If the suite is locked: naming problems go back to QA; where the suite is written a sprint at a time, a criterion no locked test names goes to QA once (`acceptance_return_to="release"`), and one still without a test is deferred ("no locked test was written for it before the release") and ships as a named cut; each deferred criterion with a locked test gets one last single-call rewrite (`LAST_TRY_ON_DEFERRED`); the suite is run; a suite that did not run, proves nothing or could not start the product fails the release.
2. Only if it passes: the project's production build (`app/stacks/production_build.py`), then the product's own unit tests (quarantined ones set aside). A failing unit test blocks unless `release_with_failing_unit_tests.may_ship` allows it (the locked suite passes and the run had already accepted them at an escalation, or release attempts are spent); then it ships recorded.
3. The handoff: `release_docs.complete_the_handoff` writes what can be written; what is still missing (README with run instructions, routes named for an HTTP product) holds the release; `release_audits.secrets_found` holds it for a credential in the code.
4. Held (any failure with `release_attempts` < 2): decision recorded, `release_attempts` += 1, `stage="technical_lead"` with the reason in `revision_comments`, plan cleared, the last sprint goes again on the judgement model. Past two holds: escalation.
5. Passed: clear command lines from code, remove the pack's untouched templates nothing loads, remove data the run's own tests wrote, then assemble `completion_gaps` (deferred criteria, reclassified tests, problems the suite was locked with, plan problems noted at dispatch, shipped failing unit tests, licence gaps, quarantined tests, unproven setup, known issues, risks recorded by reviews, reviews that were not independent, code no review read, probes that still find something at release, mutation gaps), write CI and container files (`app/stacks/release.py` from the pack's `ci_setup`, `ci_prepare` and `release_files`; never over a file the product already has), score screenshots (`SCREENSHOT_RUBRIC`), record the `release` decision, write the evidence page and hand to `complete` or `user_review_release`.

### 2.8 Escalation: the last rung

The ladder, in order, for stuck work:

1. **Fix** the defect (rewrite, candidates or agent loop; section 7).
2. **Fix again**, on the judgement model after two failed fixes or one revert (`the_build_model`).
3. **Dispute**: the QA lead judges the failing locked tests (once per failed attempt) before the sprint is planned again.
4. **Re-plan the sprint**, built on the judgement model from the first re-plan, with the cause journal and what the last attempt did in the lead's request. Best attempt kept (`best_attempt`).
5. **Escalation** (`graph/escalation.py`), which decides rather than stops:
   - `kind="design"` (architecture review never converged): auto mode builds from the latest specification and records the open findings as known risks after `specification_rulings.settle` rules on contradictions (a model's ruling whose giving side opens with a criterion id or is a criterion's text, or whose two statements name different routes or share no word, is never recorded or shown to a builder, and one recorded earlier is not shown; `specification_rulings.why_it_cannot_apply`, a `rule_could_not_apply` record, LF.1476); approval mode asks the person (`user_review_escalation`: accept or retry with guidance, resetting the revision count).
     In thorough delivery with a gating design finding open (`design_gate.holds`), the run never builds by itself: it waits at `user_review_escalation` whatever the review policy (an unattended or `auto_approve` run too - `auto_approve` answers write approvals only), recording the decision "wait for a person ..." with the open findings. The approval-timeout sweep does not answer that gate (`approval_timeouts._held_for_a_person`), and a timeout's approval reaching `escalation_accept` opens the gate again. Approve builds and records each open finding as approved by the person; Request changes revises again with the guidance, and the re-review is asked about the open findings first.
     In fast delivery (one revision, then `ONE_DESIGN_REVIEW`'s gates or the second review decide) behind `FAST_DESIGN_GATE_ON_PRODUCT_BREAKING` (LF.1524; `graph/fast_design_gate.py`), a gating finding holds the build only when it says the product cannot work, classed in one place from its own words with the specification's quotes taken out (`PHRASINGS`) and from the workflow's checks (`coverage` and `deferrals` a brief requirement, `order` the contract): it cannot start in the harness setup (a data folder nothing makes, a setting or service the setup does not give with no default, a fixed port), a brief requirement is dropped, deferred or redefined (also `brief_actions_the_text_defers` on its quotes), one user's data is open to another or authentication is skipped, or the contract the suite is written from contradicts itself on a status code or a field. A held finding gets up to `MOST_REVISIONS` (2) revisions, each followed by a real review (the gates do not stand in for it while one is open, and its check is asked again); still open after the second, it goes to the design escalation, recorded as before. Each held finding's course is a decision (`H<n> ... held as product-breaking: sent back for revision k of 2`, `resolved in review round n`, `to the design escalation`), the findings not held one decision per round naming each once; `loop.sh effects` counts held, not held and the extra revisions. Measured on 888 gating findings of 324 stored final reviews: 6 start, 29 brief, 82 contract, 0 security, 771 not held.
   - Sprint kind: with no locked criteria the run stops; if deferring the failing criteria would leave more than half of all criteria undelivered the run stops ("a run that cannot deliver most of its criteria is stopped rather than shipped"); if later sprints build on what would be deferred (`graph/foundation.py`: the product does not start, or most tests of later sprints' criteria already fail at the same step as the deferred criteria's tests, or a later sprint names it as a dependency while it passes fewer than half its own tests), the sprint is planned again once with that evidence given to the lead while re-plans remain - with what the harness measured (`foundation.the_last_measurement`, LF.1441: what is known to hold, each failing criterion's failing tests with what each said, the server's stated errors, the unit report's distinct causes, the attempt's open defects with what they printed) and the rule that a cause read from the code is a reading, not a measurement; the re-planned attempt's time budget starts at the re-plan (`foundation_replanned_at`, LF.1448) - else the run stops as not deliverable naming the foundation - never the next sprint on it (LF.1264); otherwise the failing criteria are deferred with the reason (`suite.defer`), the failed attempt is closed as accepted-at-escalation, unit failures open at that point are recorded as accepted, and the run goes to `sprint_advance` or `release`. Approval mode asks; `escalation_accept` does the same, `escalation_retry` plans the sprint again with the person's guidance and counters reset.
   - `kind="suite"` (TG.2, `graph/suite_gate.py`): thorough delivery's acceptance suite with a gating finding open at its lock. Always waits at `user_review_escalation`, recording "wait for a person ..." with the open findings and `docs/reviews/escalation.md`; not counted toward `MAX_ESCALATIONS`. The timeout sweep does not answer it and a timeout's approval opens the gate again. Approve: each open finding recorded as approved by the person, back to `acceptance_review`, which locks the suite as it stands without the QA lead reading it again. Request changes: `acceptance_author` with the open findings and the guidance.
   - **Review findings survive the escalation** (PQ.4, LF.1373, always on; `graph/review_after_escalation.py`): before the accepted state is measured, the code review is asked once, in one tool-less call on the judgement model (`code_reviewer.ONE_CALL_SYSTEM_PROMPT`), over the sprint's diff since the last accepted sprint (at most 40,000 characters), its work orders, due criteria and the specification's standards. It gates nothing. Its blocking and major findings (at most 6, less those about a later sprint's criteria) get one fix pass: one rewrite per named file (at most 3, `rewritten_fixes.fix_by_rewriting` on the build model), measured once by the fix guard (`engineering.before_a_fix` / `after_a_fix`), which puts the whole pass back if it broke what passed. Each finding becomes a known issue - "had one fix pass after the escalation, not reviewed again" (a note) when its file was rewritten and kept, "is still open after the escalation" otherwise (a known defect) - and a `code_review_after_escalation` decision records it; that decision also stops the release saying "No review read the product's code". Cost, on an accepted escalation only: one review call, and with a major finding in a named file at most three rewrite calls and the fix's two measurements.
   - More than `MAX_ESCALATIONS` (3): failed. A design escalation the person sends back (Request changes) is not counted (TG.3, LF.1371): `escalation_retry` takes it off `escalation_count` and `design_escalation_count`, so a person who keeps asking for changes is asked again rather than the run failing. Sprint escalations and approved design escalations count as before.

---

## 3. The agents and roles

### 3.1 Construction

Every role is `create_role_agent` (`app/agents/role_agent.py`) wrapping `deepagents.create_deep_agent`:

- a bounded tool set (3.2); the deepagents built-in write tools are denied at the permission layer as well as absent from the tool list;
- a structured response schema (Pydantic) per role - the answer tool - so the workflow routes on fields, not prose;
- `interrupt_on` for every high-risk tool (`HUMAN_APPROVAL_TOOLS`: `write_artifact`, `edit_artifact`, `replace_lines`, `delete_artifact`, `move_artifact`, `run_terminal_command`, `run_validation`). With `auto_approve` on, the runner answers these automatically; with it off each pauses for a person;
- a middleware chain, outermost first: compaction (keeps the stage's request - a conversation no longer than the kept tail is never shortened, and a tool result no model reply has followed is never folded, LF.1465 - a summarised conversation is summarised again once 8 messages follow the tail the summary really kept (recorded on it as `harness_kept_tail`; it was counted from the configured 15, so a tail cut to its token share of three messages needed 23 messages, ten calls, before the next summary, and one could be cut from a summarised conversation no longer than 15 messages), and at once, whatever the triggers say, when the next request - the last reply's server-reported input and output tokens and the results since at the calibrated rate - would pass the room the slot leaves once the reply's reserve (at most a third of the slot; a fix stage's 12,000 under its own trigger) is held back and summarising what lies before the kept tail could bring it back, LF.1499-LF.1500, `models/request_fit.py` - summarises the rest as the stage's work note - the summariser is told the stage's role and what its request asks (the sections it says its ask under - a work order's ask and the files it owns, the milestone a test round checks and its changed files, a review's work orders, a fixer's defect - else the text before its first heading when that is an ask, else the opening, `compaction.aim_of`, LF.1466), and the harness's own ledger of the stage from its tool calls (`agents/work_note_ledger.py`, LF.1466): the files it wrote (every one is named under Changed whatever the note says), the files written in the part summarised (an earlier Left item naming one may be done), each refusal the tools gave (a refused write dropped once the file is written after it) and each command with how it ended - the refusals and commands are also listed under every summary for the stage itself, and before a note is kept a `path:line` past its file's end, or a path naming no file in the workspace, is marked so by the harness; shown every call of the part it replaces with long results as their start and end, and asked for what was found with file and line, decided, changed and is left, never a file's text, LF.1360; the latest note is what a restart of the stage starts from, `agents/carry_over.py`, LF.1361 - and names the files it had read with their line ranges, LF.1285, and keeps how often each was read for the project memory to choose from, LF.1288, `agents/compaction.py`; a read counts when its result carries numbered lines in the read tool's two-space gutter or the older tab, LF.1399; a file read that the project memory holds whole on the call is named in the summary as held, "do not read them again", not among the files whose text is gone - the project memory rewrites that line with what it holds, `compaction.with_held_reads_named`, LF.1424; behind `FIX_STAGES_STUB_OLD_READS` (group Efficiency, off until measured, `docs/measuring.md` M-LF.1420, `agents/old_reads_stubbed.py`, LF.1420) a `defect_fix_*` stage is not summarised by a model while it can be helped otherwise: a read whose lines a later read shows again unchanged becomes one line, "read `path` lines a-b, unchanged", and at the trigger every read before the kept tail becomes such a line saying whether the file on disk still holds those lines (in steps of 10 messages, so the cache holds between steps), the stage's own words, carried reasoning, command results and refusals untouched and observation folding not run there; the model summary runs only if the request is still past the trigger, and the trigger follows the fix stage's 12,000-token reply cap when `FIX_STAGE_REPLY_CAP` is on (59,020 instead of 43,020 tokens on a 91,776-token window); an `old_reads_stubbed` record once per visit); transcript; verify-after-write; batch file claims; research share; fresh-conversation reset; room for reads (behind `READS_SIZED_TO_THE_ROOM`, off until measured, `docs/measuring.md` M18, LF.1289: keeps the room the stage's next request has - the last reply's reported input tokens against the input ceiling, less the tool results since - and a `read_file` naming no range of a file over 80 lines whose numbered text is past that room is answered by the backend with the file's outline, each definition with its first and last line, and how to read a range; a range read never changes, `agents/room_for_reads.py`); gathering (a stage on the judgement model gathers on the build model, `agents/gathering.py`); text-tool-call repair; workspace path normaliser; line batches; environment contract; workspace digest (a map of the workspace with line counts, purpose line, interface, marking harness-owned files; its closing line of files and commands already in the conversation leaves out a read answered by the project memory's pointer or taken out by `FIX_STAGES_STUB_OLD_READS`, and after a summary says its calls are counted from the summary, LF.1424); project memory (the project's documents and files handed over current at the start of each call - at the start of the stage's request, with its line of held files at the request's end, not on a note before it, LF.1574 - a read of one already held answered with a pointer; the opening request's line of held files names them "as they were when this stage began" and points to the end-of-request note that names any changed since, LF.1482; a test runner's or coverage tool's report - `test-results*.xml`, `junit*.xml`, `TEST-*.xml`, or any XML whose root is `testsuites`, `testsuite` or `coverage` - is never held or named, LF.1389; a fixer, and a builder opening on code an earlier stage wrote, get the specification, the acceptance criteria and (a builder) the sprint plan, each long one as a digest of the sections its request points at, the other plans named and the product's code with the rest of the budget; with `REQUEST_BUDGET_FOR_EVERY_STAGE` on, a stage whose request and memory are past its input budget gets the memory within what the budget leaves, long documents as digests, 7.3a; a review - security, advice, code, milestone - whose files today's request and memory would not show all of gets them held first and numbered, the plans as digests after them, and its request names them as held instead of repeating them or asking for a read of each (`agents/review_files.py`, LF.1284; a product whose files fit is sent as before); the same for the other stages measured re-reading what they work from - the QA lead's review of the suite (its files), the technical lead (the product's code and the due locked tests, `agents/lead_files.py`) and a test round's testing stage (the milestone's changed files) - LF.1286; past the room, every code file not held whole is in an outline of its definitions and tests, each with its first and last line, the documents first, then the outline, then the files held whole, and the request says to read the lines needed by range (`analysis/outline.py`, LF.1287); at each summary the files held whole are chosen again - those the stage wrote, latest first, then those it read most, never a throwaway it wrote for itself (`file_kinds.is_scratch`, a `tmp-` repro script, LF.1400) - and the request's line of held files rewritten, the suite's author and the builders included once the product no longer fits, a builder past its room opening on the outline too (`review_files.chosen_again`, `WRITING_STAGES`, LF.1288), and a fix stage with `FIXERS_HOLD_WHAT_THEY_READ` on (group Efficiency, off until measured, `docs/measuring.md` M-LF.1406, `agents/fixers_hold_what_they_read.py`, a `fixer_memory_chosen_again` record each time, LF.1406); between summaries the memory stays byte for byte; once the project is past the memory's room, the files the request's work bears on come right after the documents - those a section of its ask names (a section naming more than eight files is a listing, not the ask), what they import, the files defining at least two names the ask uses, and the tests of the criteria it names (not a later sprint's) - and the harness's own kept files after every product file, `CANONICAL_MEMORY` or not (`project_memory.the_work_s_files`, LF.1468; the request is the stage's own ask - the opening turn's user messages less the harness's notes, a restarted stage's carry-over, the empty-project notice and the workspace listing, `project_memory.the_stage_s_ask` - by which the memory is also sized and a review's held files read, LF.1565; within the room the order is the canonical one, so siblings share bytes); a workflow document the request quotes whole under a heading naming its path (`--- ... (/docs/x.md) ---`) is neither held nor digested but named as quoted, and a read of it, other than a few lines by range, is answered with a pointer to the request (`project_memory.quoted_whole_in`, LF.1469; a lead's re-plan under `LEAD_REPLAN_REQUEST_SMALLER` too, LF.1566); a document quoted cut short - its section carries an excerpt's note or a cut marker (`text_budget.EXCERPT_MARK`, `TRUNCATED_MARK`) - is not quoted whole: the memory keeps it and a read of it reads it (LF.1573); a read of a held file on a call that withheld reading is passed on to the refusal that call announced, not answered with the pointer (`settled_answer.withheld_now`, LF.1405); when the call decides reads file by file (`settled_answer.reads_decided_per_file`, LF.1517) a read of a file the memory holds whole and unchanged, or a document the request quotes whole, is refused by name with where its text is, and any other read goes on); plugin instructions (3.5); filesystem layer; edits-together; repeated-query guard; tool result bound (a single result that by itself would push the stage's next request past its room keeps its start and end, never less than a quarter of the room, with a pointer: a read is read again by range, any other result is saved whole in the journal for `read_command_output`, `agents/tool_result_bound.py`, LF.1502); settled-answer guard (ends a stage once it has answered and stopped progressing; budget 40 calls; the end of a reply's reasoning that reached its cap - its last 3,000 characters from a paragraph or sentence, kept by `models/reply_salvage.py` whether or not the reply was salvaged - is appended to every later request of the stage visit as "What you worked out", so no summary takes it, a later capped reply replacing it; and once thinking is off for the rest of the visit - two replies lost to reasoning, a fix stage's two salvaged replies, or `LONG_REASONING_BEFORE_A_READ`'s rule - and the model can be asked without it, each call is offered only the tools that change a file or run a command, with its answer, and told why; a stage with no writer keeps its tools; a read sent anyway is decided per file as in LF.1517 - a first read runs, what the conversation or the memory holds is refused - unless the stage is stalled, on its last calls or ignoring the notices, LF.1527; `agents/worked_out.py`, LF.1421); optional task list (`HARNESS_TASK_LIST`);
- a checkpointer, so a paused approval resumes the inner agent (`resume_with_decision`) rather than restarting it.

A role whose document the workflow renders has no write tool: the model returns its structured answer and a template renders `docs/*.md`. Reasoning ("thinking") is controlled per model and stage (`agents/thinking.py`): fast delivery runs the build model with reasoning off, a fix reasons as configured, replies that spend the whole reply cap reasoning are salvaged from their own reasoning (`models/reply_salvage.py`). Behind `LONG_REASONING_BEFORE_A_READ` (`agents/deliberated_reads.py`), the reply after one that reasoned 8,000 tokens or more and then only read goes without thinking, for that one reply.

### 3.2 Tool lists

| Role | Tools |
|---|---|
| Read-only roles: product manager, tools and models architect, scrum master, technical lead, architecture reviewer, acceptance reviewer (QA lead), suite dispute, code reviewer, security reviewer, milestone reviewer, advice reviewer, specialist advisor | workspace readers `ls`, `read_file`, `glob`, `grep` (deepagents' filesystem backend wrapped so `.agent-home` is hidden, `agents/workspace_backend.py`); `changed_since_sprint`; the structured answer tool. `can_write=False`. |
| Principal architect, stack selector | the readers plus `look_up_package`, `read_docs`, `search_the_web` and `read_web_page` (when a search is configured; by the model's measured verdict, `research` in their place or none, 8.3), and MCP tools the owner configures (`HARNESS_MCP_SERVERS`) or an official library server found in the MCP Registry. The architect's reading is also done for it before it starts (`agents/research_brief.py`). |
| Specialists (`ui_ux`, `database`, `backend`, `testing`, `documentation`, `security_reliability`) and QA when writing the suite | readers; writers `write_artifact`, `edit_artifact` (find/replace), `replace_lines` (by line range; offered when `line_edits_offered()`), `delete_artifact`, `move_artifact`; `run_terminal_command` (one program with arguments, no shell, role allowlist); `read_command_output` (a long output by reference); `look_up_package`; `module_exports` (what an installed package really exports); `read_docs`; `search_the_web`/`read_web_page` (by the model's measured verdict, `research` in their place or none, 8.3); MCP tools; `use_runtime` (a runtime version the image lacks, per project); `look_at_page` (the running page's accessibility tree, errors and failed requests, for products with a page). The `testing` role also gets `run_validation`. Optional: `ask_specification` (`ASK_SPECIFICATION`). The `code_graph` tool and its switch `CODE_GRAPH_TOOL` are removed (KG.10, LF.1535: offered about 3,500 times, called none); what the code graph knows reaches a stage through the requests the harness writes (the neighbourhood, the slice, the codebase map). |

`LEAN_TOOL_LISTS` (a switch, on) leaves out the four tools almost never called (`search_the_web`, `read_web_page`, `use_runtime`, `ask_specification`) except where a stage's request asks for one; a switch whose only effect is one of those tools (`ASK_SPECIFICATION`) then reads as off, in its own `enabled()` and so in every run's provenance and KPI row, since it acts nowhere (`stage_tools.leaves_out`, LF.1431); `changed_since_sprint` is offered only once a sprint has been accepted; a read-only role on an empty workspace is offered `read_file` alone (`agents/stage_tools.py`). `TOOL_LISTS_BY_ROLE_KIND` (a switch, on; TR.7, LF.1541, `agents/role_tool_lists.py`) gives each writing specialist its role kind's own list, as a fixer has its own (FX.3): a builder the readers, `write_artifact`, `edit_artifact` (`replace_lines` where line edits are on), `delete_artifact`, `run_terminal_command` and `read_command_output`; a writer of tests (the testing role: the unit-test writer, the acceptance author, the test round, a stack's setup) those and `run_validation`; either keeps `module_exports` where the run offers it (a stack's guidance names it), a tool its request asks for (`use_runtime` in a setup no pack covers), the owner's MCP tools and `ask_specification` when its own switch is on. Left out: `move_artifact` (the writing rules then drop its line), `read_docs`, `research` or the web pair, `look_up_package`, `look_at_page` and `changed_since_sprint` - 9 calls in 4,260 writer requests, about 6,200 characters of schema; the stack selector and the architect keep the registry and documentation tools. The run records once per role kind what was left out (`tool_list_chosen`), and `loop.sh effects` gives the writers' tool-schema characters. `STABLE_TOOLS` (built in) keeps one tool list for a whole stage visit and refuses a withheld tool's call instead of removing it, so a server's prompt cache stays valid. On a call where only the answer is allowed, the request names the answer tool as its `tool_choice` with the tool list unchanged, on a server whose model diagnosis found a named choice answered with that tool (`structured_answers` "forced", `models/named_answer.py`, set by `diagnostics.apply`; PA.16, LF.1388); elsewhere the call goes as before (`required`, or `auto` where forcing is refused while reasoning). Measured: 29-34 withheld-tool refusals a medium run; the served model's diagnosis called `read_file` first 3 of 3 times under `required` and answered 3 of 3 when named. The LM Studio server on :1234 refuses a named choice (diagnosed `required`), so there nothing changes. The notice of tools still listed but refused names every refused tool, or, when fewer are still allowed, says "every tool except" those (`settled_answer.stable_tools_notice`; it named the first 12 alphabetically, LF.1478). It is the one place a call says what may be called: the other withholding notices give only their reason and name a way forward only through a tool the stage is offered (`write_artifact` for a refused edit), and a refusal of a withheld tool lists the tools of the stage's next call where the budget decides them - on its last two calls the writers, past them none - else those of the call that refused it (LF.1518; a live fixer was told "`grep` and the directory listings still work" and "Everything else still works" beside a list refusing all but three tools, and had no listing tool). Once a stage has been refused three redundant reads or pointed at the project memory eight times, its reads are decided file by file where nothing else withholds reading on the call: the read tool stays listed, a read of a file the project memory holds whole is refused by `project_memory`, a read of the same lines whose numbered result the conversation the call sends still holds - read since its last summary, not folded, the file not written since - is refused by `settled_answer`, each naming the file and where its text is, and any other read runs and counts against the call budget; withheld by another rung on the call (the budget's last calls, a stall, thinking off, the read itself refused four times) the tool goes whole, as before (LF.1517; 51 of 85 whole-tool refusals over 26 runs asked for a file the request no longer held). Each read made while reading is withheld leaves a `read_while_withheld` record of how it was decided (held whole, still in the conversation, document quoted whole, withheld whole by another rule, or ran), and each write on the calls the budget keeps for the answer a `write_at_the_budget` record (run on a last call, or refused); `loop.sh effects` counts both (`agents/withheld_records.py`). Records only.

### 3.3 The roles
