| Role | Prompt/module | Model | Output | May write |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Product Manager | `agents/product_manager.py` | planning | `ProductManagerOutput` | nothing (document rendered) |
| Tools and Models Architect | `agents/tools_models.py` | planning | `ToolsAndModelsOutput` | nothing |
| Stack Selector | `agents/stack_selector.py` | planning | `StackSelectorOutput` | nothing |
| Specialist advisors `{role}_advisor` | `agents/specialist_advice.py` | judgement (build model in fast delivery) | numbered advice items | nothing |
| Principal Architect | `agents/principal_architect.py` | judgement | `TechnicalSpecificationOutput` with ADRs, file plan, contracts, interface design and contract | nothing |
| Senior Architect Reviewer | `agents/architecture_reviewer.py` | judgement | `ArchitectureReviewOutput` | nothing |
| QA engineer (testing specialist, `writing_the_suite`) | `agents/specialists.py`, `agents/acceptance_reviewer.py`, `acceptance/requests.py` | judgement (default model in fast delivery) | the suite files | acceptance directory only (3.4) |
| QA lead | `agents/acceptance_reviewer.py`, `agents/suite_dispute.py` | judgement (`reviews_without_reasoning` for the review) | approve/findings; dispute rulings | nothing |
| Scrum Master | `agents/scrum_master.py` | planning | `SprintPlanOutput` | nothing |
| Technical Lead | `agents/technical_lead.py` | planning | `MilestonePlanOutput` (work orders) | nothing |
| Specialists (six roles) | `agents/specialists.py` | build model; judgement after failures or on a retried sprint | `SpecialistOutput` | their work order's files |
| Test engineer (testing specialist) | `agents/specialists.py` | judgement | defects | test files only in a test round; nothing else |
| Fixers (the defect's owning specialist) | `agents/specialists.py` (`fixing=True`: `agents/lean_fixer.py`'s prompt and few tools) + fix request | build model with reasoning on; judgement after 2 failed fixes or a revert | `SpecialistOutput` | files the defect and its mode allow |
| Code Reviewer | `agents/code_reviewer.py` | judgement | findings | nothing |
| Security Reviewer | `agents/security_reviewer.py` | judgement | `SecurityReviewOutput` | nothing |
| Advice reviewers | `agents/advice_review.py`, `agents/advice_reviews_together.py`, `agents/specialist_domain_review.py`, `agents/page_facts.py` | judgement | adopted advice not built; with `SPECIALIST_DOMAIN_REVIEW`, defects in their own domain. Two or more specialists with items due whose items fit a quarter of the request room are one call (`advice_review_together`, PA.9 rest, LF.1560, always on): each specialist's items under its own heading, the sprint and its files once, the answer split back by item id; a UI/UX reviewer that is shown screenshots, and every role while `SPECIALIST_DOMAIN_REVIEW` is on, stay their own call | nothing |
| Milestone Reviewer | `agents/milestone_reviewer.py` | judgement | accepted / changes_required / blocked with findings | nothing |

**Page facts for a UI/UX specialist that cannot see** (LF.1323, plan SP.4, always on, `agents/page_facts.py`): the page check every browser suite run starts with (`stacks/packs/_javascript.page_check`, the harness's Playwright, the same pass that keeps `page-desktop.png` and `page-phone.png`) also measures, at 1280px and 375px (`PAGE_FACTS`), each text colour on its composited background as sRGB with its contrast ratio and whether it is below WCAG AA (4.5:1, 3:1 for large text), the outermost elements sticking out past the viewport and how far the page scrolls sideways, the stylesheet rules that match no element as the page first loads, the rules that set the outline to none, the form fields with no label (or only a placeholder), the custom properties the loaded stylesheets declare (`declared`), the same facts again at 1280px in the dark colour scheme (`facts.dark`, LF.1377), the accessibility tree at the phone's width, and then presses Tab up to 8 times at 1280px recording each control reached and whether it shows an outline or a ring (`FOCUSED`); all into `facts` of `test-results/page-load.json`. When the UI/UX advice reviewer's model does not read images (`models/vision.the_reviewer_reads_images`), its request gets these facts with the layout check's overlaps, small targets and serious accessibility findings, at most 3,600 characters (the accessibility tree cut first); nothing when the page check measured no page. A reviewer that reads images still gets the screenshots instead.

**Mechanical interface checks** (LF.1377, plan PQ.5, always on, `analysis/interface_style_checks.py`, raised by the page check with `interface_defects` at each test round of a page product): from the page check's facts and the product's files, reusing the quality score's static checks (`product_quality.interface_part`): (d) the pack's token stylesheet is loaded - one of its custom properties is declared on the rendered page, else (an older page check) its file is named by the page's code; (b) text reaches WCAG AA on its background in both colour schemes, from the computed styles (light pairs left to the accessibility check when it already reports contrast); (a) every class and id a stylesheet styles is used by the markup or code, and every class the page's code sets now and then (`classList.add/toggle`, a condition choosing it, `class:x`, `[class.x]`, `:class`) is styled; (c) no raw colour in a stylesheet where the pack ships tokens; (e) no focus outline removed without a replacement (with the Tab facts naming controls that show none). Gating, as `major` `ui_ux` defects raised every round while found: an unloaded token stylesheet (`Design tokens: ...` - the whole designed floor goes with it, and the fix is one import) and body text under 4.5:1 in either scheme (`Text contrast: ...`, with where its elements are styled). Everything else is one bundled `minor` defect (`Styling faults: ...`, at most 6 lines of evidence per check, each `file:line`): it gets one fix, and raised again after a failed fix the test round accepts it as a recorded risk (`set_aside_after_one_try`, reason `SET_ASIDE`) rather than holding the sprint.

Common prompt machinery: `agents/lean_prompts.py` (a shorter prompt for a model measured strong), `agents/shared_facts.py` (one account of the facts a sprint is built and judged under, handed to builders and judges alike), `agents/scope_discipline.py` (one rule against inflating scope), `agents/severity.py` and `agents/finding_category.py` (findings that must show evidence to block), `agents/stable_prefix.py`, `agents/stable_memory_order.py` (keep the request prefix byte-stable for server caches), `agents/carry_over.py` (what a dying attempt learned goes to the next on a fresh conversation), `agents/stage_memory.py` (counters that survive approval pauses). `agents/normal_form.py` (LF.1317): one sentence - where the product groups or counts values compared in a normal form (lower case, trimmed, accents folded), it reports each group in that normal form, not the spelling it read first, unless the brief says which spelling to show - told to the principal architect, the backend and UI/UX builders (and so their fixers) and each product file's draft; the suite's writer and QA lead are told a test of such a grouping feeds two spellings of one value and checks the reported key is the normal form, not only that the count merged (`acceptance/requests.py`).
Common prompt machinery: `agents/lean_prompts.py` (a shorter prompt for a model measured strong), `agents/shared_facts.py` (one account of the facts a sprint is built and judged under, handed to builders and judges alike), `agents/scope_discipline.py` (one rule against inflating scope), `agents/severity.py` and `agents/finding_category.py` (findings that must show evidence to block), `agents/stable_prefix.py`, `agents/stable_memory_order.py` (keep the request prefix byte-stable for server caches), `agents/carry_over.py` (what a dying attempt learned goes to the next on a fresh conversation, its last work note first, LF.1361; spent when the stage answers - the test round's own update carries that, LF.1467), `agents/stage_memory.py` (counters that survive approval pauses). `agents/normal_form.py` (LF.1317): one sentence - where the product groups or counts values compared in a normal form (lower case, trimmed, accents folded), it reports each group in that normal form, not the spelling it read first, unless the brief says which spelling to show - told to the principal architect, the backend and UI/UX builders (and so their fixers) and each product file's draft; the suite's writer and QA lead are told a test of such a grouping feeds two spellings of one value and checks the reported key is the normal form, not only that the count merged (`acceptance/requests.py`).

### 3.4 What each role may write: the single-writer rules

Enforced in `app/tools/repository.py` and `app/stacks/contract.py`, not by prompts:

| Rule | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| The **locked suite** is QA's alone. Any write tool refuses it. A command that changes it anyway is undone before the next run. QA, while writing or repairing, may write only the acceptance directory (the product is not QA's to build). | `_the_harness_s_own_or_the_locked_suite`, `suite.is_protected`, `what_a_command_may_not_change` |
| **`.harness/`** is the harness's record; no stage may write it. | same |
| **Kept files** (runner configuration the pack owns: `playwright.config.*`, `pytest.ini`, `acceptance/conftest.py`, the serve script, test setup) and kept manifest scripts and lines are refused to agents and put back if a stage changed them. | `contract.enforce`, `StackPack.kept_files/kept_scripts/kept_lines` |
| **`docs/`** holds only workflow-rendered documents. | repository write guard |
| **A work order's files** belong to its specialist; a path two work orders both list has no single owner and is staggered. A stage refused outside its own files three times is told to stop. A change refused in another work order's file is the harness's hand-off to the owner (LF.1548, both modes): the first refusal records the file, the owner and the attempted change (cut to 300 characters, `.harness/foreign-file-handoffs.json`, cleared with every milestone plan) and says so; the second is one short line ("Recorded for <owner>: <change>. Do not send it again; finish your own files.") and from then on the stage's writes to that path are withheld for the rest of the stage (answered before the tool runs, counted by the stall rule as nothing new). The change is added to the refused stage's `blockers` when it hands over; the owner gets it on its next model call when it is running or starts later (a message in its own conversation, once), else, when every builder has finished and before CI, on its next visit through the `unfinished_orders` note (once a sprint attempt). Ledger events `foreign file change recorded for its owner`, `... delivered to its running owner`, `... sent to its owner's next visit`; `loop.sh effects` counts them and the refusals. | `record_work_order_files`, `_outside_its_own_files`, `tools/foreign_file_handoffs.py`, `settled_answer.withhold_writes_to` |
| The **testing work order writes tests, not product code**; its first refused change to a product file is recorded as its blocker by the harness (the file and what the test needed, `.harness/testing-blockers.json`), added to its `blockers` when it hands over, and sent to the builder that owns the file (LF.1310); a second refusal of that file in the stage is one short line. A test round reports a product fault in `defects`. | `_a_tester_changing_the_product`, `tools/testing_blockers.py` |
| **A stage is asked to fix only what it may write** (LF.1345, PA.8): after a stage answers, the errors the checks find in the files it touched (language service, compiler, a held declaration dropped) are split by the same guards its file tools apply (`why_the_stage_may_not_write`: the harness's records and the locked suite, another work order's file, a testing stage or a fix held to tests and product code). Errors in its own files are put to it once, as before; an error in a file it may not write is added to its answer's `blockers` by the harness (a testing work order's also recorded for the builder of the file, as LF.1310's refused changes are) and the stage ends with its answer. Ledger event `post-answer error in another's file kept as a blocker`. A budget notice counts an answer sent through the answer tool as written (LF.1346). | `graph/its_own_to_fix.py`, `observability/transcript.asked_for` |
| **Fixers of one batch never write the same file**: a fixer's first write claims the file; another's write is refused with what the file needs. | `graph/batch_file_claims.py` |
| A **whole-file write over another stage's file this stage has not read** is refused until it reads it. | `_refuse_a_blind_overwrite_of_another_stage_s_file` |
| A first fix of a failing **unit test** beside a green locked suite writes test files only. Behind `UNIT_TEST_FIX_BY_FAILURE` (PA.15, LF.1572, group "Unit test signal", off in code, on in compose) the failure chooses the side first: one that reached no assertion (each language's runner wording, `stacks/languages.py` `assertion_failed`), names no stand-in (in the failure or a failing test's file) and locates its error in an application file of the workspace (a trace frame, a compiler diagnostic) allows product code at once, and the fixer is told where the error is raised ("The failure is raised in the product's code"); an assertion that fails, a stand-in or an error located only in test files stays tests-only. | `tools/tests_only_writes.py` (`TESTS_ONLY_WRITES`, built in), `analysis/unit_failure_side.py` |
| A **unit test with a stand-in checks only its own code** (LF.1312): the testing role, each test file's draft and a fixer held to test files are told that a test that replaces a component with a stand-in (mock, stub, fake, patched name) checks only what the code under test does, and a rule the replaced component enforces is tested in its own test or without the stand-in (the Java pack's guide says it of `@WebMvcTest`). A fixer of failing unit tests is shown the evidence when a failing test expected a refusal (a 4xx, an error) and got a success, its file stands a component in, and a passing locked acceptance test refuses the same input: "the product already refuses this ... the stand-in hides the rule". Stand-in declarations and runner messages are read per language (`stacks/languages.py`: `stand_ins`, `refusal_not_given`); counted by `loop.sh effects`. | `agents/stand_in_rule.py`, `analysis/stand_in_tests.py` |
| A **unit test starts from an empty store of its own** (LF.1316): the testing role, each test file's draft (with its language's way: `stacks/languages.py` `fresh_store`) and a fixer held to test files are told that a test that stores data starts from a fresh temporary data folder (the `DATA_DIR`-style setting) or a store cleared before each test, and never counts on what another test stored. A fixer of failing unit tests is shown the evidence when a failing test's message shows a count or list larger than expected (`more_than_expected`) and carries values written only in other tests of its file (tests read by `test_starts`; not its own, not the shared setup, not a value the product's code writes): the records that leaked, the tests that stored them, the file's other tests whose counts grew. The runner's report is read first; a failure's listing keeps what came as well as what was expected (`ci._STATES_THE_ERROR`). Counted by `loop.sh effects`. | `agents/own_store_rule.py`, `analysis/shared_store_tests.py` |
| A **layered setup stage** writes only the files it was asked for. | `SETUP_SCOPE_GUARD` |
| A file its language's parser rejects, a leaked model control token, a hand-written lock file, a test switched off, a hand-built DOM, an edit that goes backwards again: **refused at the write**. | `_refuse_*` helpers; `analysis/grammar_parse.py` (Python `ast`, `json`, `node --check`, TypeScript parser, tree-sitter grammars measured exact for Java, Go, Rust, Ruby, PHP, Lua, a CSS grammar; brace count only for unmeasured languages) |
| Paths stay inside the workspace and project directory. | repository guard |

### 3.5 Plugin instructions and steering

`app/plugins/` appends framework- and language-specific guidance to each agent's system prompt from the run's chosen stack (`middleware.py`, store `registry.py`, content `seed.py`). A framework the store has never seen gets instructions written once by the model and stored. Approved lessons for the stack (`lessons.py`) are appended in a fixed order so the prefix stays stable; nothing an agent wrote reaches a prompt unapproved. The same mechanism carries the **owner's steering notes** (`app/server/steering.py`, `.harness/steering.json`): a note added mid-run is read by the stage in progress on its next model call, and the transcript records which call read it first. With `STEERING_AT_THE_TAIL` (bundle `PREMIUM_MODEL_TUNING`) the note goes after the transcript as the last message instead of in the system message, keeping the prompt cache valid. Per-model guidance (`app/models/guidance.py`) is a catalogue of `guide:<id>` rows, each its own switch, chosen per model by measured tier.

---

## 4. State, documents and the workspace

### 4.1 Workflow state

`WorkflowGraphState` (`workflow.py`, a `TypedDict`) is the controller's scratchpad; `app/state.WorkflowState` is the separate audit contract for external consumers. Fields by purpose:

| Group | Fields |
|---|---|
| Routing | `stage`, `error`, `pending_approval`, `pending_inner_thread_id`, `pending_decision`, `inner_thread_ids`, `review_policy`, `reviews`, `pending_review`, `pending_review_decision`, `revision_comments` |
| Stage outputs | `product_intake`, `tools_models`, `stack_selection`, `stack_pack`, `specialist_advice`, `technical_specification`, `architecture_review`, `sprint_plan`, `milestone_plan`, `specialist_outputs` (merged per task, reset by an empty update), `remediation` (the `RemediationCycle`), `milestone_review`, `security_review` |
| Counters | `architecture_revision_count`, `specification_repair_count`, `acceptance_round`, `current_sprint_index`, `sprint_replan_count`, `milestone_revision_count`, `test_round`, `defect_cursor`, `suite_repairs`, `suite_reclassified_*`, `escalation_count`, `design_escalation_count`, `release_attempts`, `structured_retries` |
| Sprint memory | `sprint_attempts`, `sprint_earlier_plans`, `sprint_findings`, `carried_forward`, `lessons`, `nothing_left_but_carried`, `sprint_best_history`, `disputed_sprints`, `dispute_returns_to`, `dispute_reason`, `work_order_problems`, `unready_plan`, `best_unready_plan` |
| Fix machinery | `fix_batch`, `fix_batch_started`, `fix_started`, `fixes_tried`, `fixes_one_at_a_time`, `fix_base_commit`, `review_defects`, `review_advice`, `ci_report`, `ci_batch_commit` |
| Parallel | `specialist_pauses`, `queued_decisions`, `reviews_in_flight`, `review_results` |
| Release | `completion_gaps`, `known_issues`, `unit_failures_accepted_at_an_escalation`, `mutation_gaps`, `mutation_probed`, `escalation`, `decisions` |

Reducers: `specialist_outputs`, `specialist_pauses`, `queued_decisions` and `review_results` use `_merge_or_reset` (merge many workers' writes in one superstep; an empty update means start again).

### 4.2 Checkpoints

LangGraph checkpoints the state per superstep (`graph/checkpointing.py`): SQLite under `DATA_DIR` or Postgres (`checkpointer_type`). Deserialisation is pinned to an explicit allowlist of the harness's Pydantic models; a new model stored in state must be added to it. Each stage's inner agent has its own thread id namespaced under the run's thread id. A visit's conversation is written down when it starts and crossed out when it returns, so a stage a worker restart killed carries on in its own conversation (`graph/abandoned_threads.py`).

### 4.3 The workspace

Each run owns a git repository, `DATA_DIR/workspaces/<tenant>/workspace-<run-id>/`, with the product in a **project directory** the intake names once (`.project-directory`; `pin_the_directory_the_product_names`). A product uploaded at the root is moved into it (`stacks/seeded_product.py`). The harness's own HOME for package caches is `.agent-home` inside the workspace (hidden from agent listings).

Commits: one per batch (`ci.commit`), one per accepted sprint with a tag per sprint (`tools/version_control.py`), stage snapshots under `refs/harness/stages/` beside the branch (neither HEAD nor the index moves), best-checkpoint commits during a fix (`graph/best_checkpoint.py`), best-attempt commits (`graph/best_attempt.py`). The release archive keeps git history and leaves out dependency folders, build output and harness files.

### 4.4 Documents a run writes (`docs/`)

Rendered by Jinja templates in `app/doc_templates/templates/` from structured answers, so their shape does not depend on the model: `product_intake.md`, `tools_and_models.md`, `technology_stack.md`, `technical_specification.md`, `architecture_review.md`, `acceptance_criteria.md`, `acceptance_tests.md`, `sprint_plan.md`, `milestone_plan.md`, `milestone_review.md`, `security_review.md`, `sprint_N_summary.md` and the Sprint Record (`app/sprint/record.py`: backlog and outcome, each defect with its cause, fix and verifying command, findings and dispositions, carried-forward items, lessons), `decision_log.md` (`app/decisions.py`, records `DR-n` with point, question, options, decision, rationale, decided_by, affects; also in state and `.harness/decisions.json` whole, and as a `decision` transcript record with its decision and rationale, LF.1432), `reviews/` (escalation page, `release-evidence.md`, `sprint-N-evidence.md`, review pages), `stage_summaries/`, `third-party-licences.md` (release audits). The release adds the handoff (README, API summary) and the product archive moves process documents to `docs/process/`.

### 4.5 The `.harness/` folder

Agents never see it and no write tool may change it. Principal contents (from `acceptance/suite.py`, `stacks/contract.py`, `ci.py` and the modules named):

| File | Holds |
|---|---|
| `stack-pack.json` | the project contract: pack, commands, report paths, kept files |
| `acceptance.json` | numbered criteria, deferrals, change requests, coverage state |
| `acceptance-snapshot/`, `acceptance-locks/vN/` (+ `vN.diff`) | the locked suite with checksums, every locked version |
| `ci.json` | per-test snapshots (`accepted`, `latest`) |
| `decisions.json`, `steering.json`, `answers.json`, `seeded.json`, `seed-moved.json` | decisions, steering notes, intake answers, seeding records |
| `fix-journal.json`, `kept_candidates.json`, `best-attempt.json`, `fix-round-before.json`, `fix-profiles.json` | what was tried on each cause, reverted fixes kept as candidates, the best attempt, the round's starting fingerprint, each fix stage's defect tests as it found them (LF.1272) |
| `contract-probe.json`, `order_probe.json`, `production-build.json`, `start_breaks.json`, `check-findings.jsonl`, `dispute-outcomes.jsonl`, `screenshot-rubric.json` | probe coverage, probe and build results, every finding a suite check raised, dispute outcomes |
| `project-graph.json`, `specification_rulings.json`, `unit_tests_that_disagree.json`, `quarantined_unit_tests.json`, `equivalent_mutants.json`, `dependency-gate.json`, `runtimes.json`, `mcp-official.json`, `stage-threads.json` | code graph, rulings, quarantined unit tests, remembered equivalent mutants, install gate record, pinned runtimes, MCP registry result, open stage conversations |

A terminal state other than a clean release writes a loud marker at the workspace root (`graph/terminal_state.py`); a clean release clears it.

### 4.6 The defect ledger, transcripts, rewind

- **Defect ledger** (`observability/defect_ledger.py`): one `defect` record per event in the run's transcript - raised, raised again, bundled, fixed, reverted, repaired forward, best checkpoint, left open, lookup, rewrite after surrender - keyed by round and index; `scripts/loop/defects.py` reads it back. A defect carries `kind`, small `evidence` records and a `cause_key` (`app/sprint/cause.py`): two defects with keys are one problem exactly when the keys are equal.
- **Transcript** (`observability/transcript.py`): the complete unclipped record of what each stage was told, replied and did, JSON Lines at `DATA_DIR/transcripts/<run_id>.jsonl`, including notices appended by middleware, each resume after a restart and which call read each steering note.
- **Rewind** (`workflow.rewind_points`, `rewind_to`): each time a stage was entered is a point (the checkpoint just before it). Rewinding makes that checkpoint the run's latest on a branch (history kept), restores the workspace from the snapshot taken for that checkpoint (`restore_snapshot`, refused when none was taken) and clears pause state. Only the start of a stage can be rewound to, and only a paused run.
- **Resume**: an orphaned or paused run resumes from the last checkpoint; the entry router picks the node.

---

## 5. Stack packs and the language table

### 5.1 What a pack is

A pack (`StackPack`, `app/stacks/__init__.py`) holds exactly what differs between kinds of product in a language: layout, install, unit and acceptance commands, report locations, how a test drives the product (a browser for a page, HTTP for a service, a process for a CLI, an import for a library). The workflow around it knows no language. A pack scaffolds because on 46 measured runs 63% of reported defects concerned tests and their setup, none of which needs judgement.

### 5.2 Pack fields

| Field group | Fields |
|---|---|
| Identity | `name`, `title`, `kind` (`browser_app`, `http_api`, `fullstack`, `cli`, `library`), `language`, `frameworks`, `frontend_frameworks`, `stack` |
| Scaffold | `files` (with `{{project}}`, `{{title}}`, `{{package}}`), `install`, `smoke_files` (one unit, one acceptance test, removed once proven), `warm_up` (network commands once tests exist), `fetches_while_building`, `process_memory`, `server_wait_ms` |
| Commands and reports | `unit_test`, `acceptance_test`, `unit_report`, `acceptance_report` (JUnit XML), `unit_dir`, `acceptance_dir`, `unit_pattern`, `acceptance_pattern`, `typecheck`, `production_build`, `list_routes`, `how_it_is_used`, `evidence_dir`, `runtime_report` |
| Kept state | `kept_files`, `kept_scripts`, `kept_lines`, `required_exports`, `required_signatures`, `entrypoint`/`entrypoints` (the stubs the drafted product must replace), `wired_by_imports`, `relative_import_extension` |
| Guidance | `guide` (how tests are written, shown to every writing stage), `acceptance_guide` (to QA), `design_facts` (stated to the architect and reviewer), `data_store`, `known_errors`, `golden_example`, `framework_files`, `proven_code` |
| Checks | `check_families`, `test_bugs`, `locators_nobody_sees`, `locators_that_pick_one`, `locators_that_do_not_exist`, `locators_by_a_missing_name`, `finds_by_name`, many callables that read a test's text (contradicting locators, tests that open no page, storage before page open, unawaited helpers, positions asserted, near misses, assertion names) |
| Release and sandbox | `ci_setup`, `ci_prepare`, `release_files`, `sandbox_image` (one image holding toolchain, runners, Node and browsers, `packs/_sandbox.py`), `unit_mirrors`, `unit_dom`, `code_runs_in`, `data_dir_env` |

Derived: `probe_runner` ("playwright" when the pack ships a Playwright config, "pytest" when the kept conftest starts the service) and `coverage` (what the harness checks for a product on this pack).

### 5.3 Selection and the contract

`choose_pack(stack, text, rationale)` tries the packs in `packs.ALL_PACKS` order and returns the first whose language, product kind and framework fit (`_framework_fits`, `_frontend_fits`, `product_kind`); a stack naming a component framework fits only a pack built with it. A stack the harness aligns to the pack gets its fields corrected (`aligned_stack`). `scaffold` writes the pack to the project directory, runs the install, writes the smoke files, runs the unit and acceptance commands through the same sandbox path every later stage uses, reads the JUnit reports, then removes the smoke tests and reports. Only a scaffold whose smoke tests pass is kept (retried whole once). The result is written to `.harness/stack-pack.json` (`contract.write`). Every later stage reads it (`contract.the_pack`, `describe`); `contract.enforce` puts back kept files, scripts and lines a stage changed (a refusal costs a turn and teaches nothing; putting back keeps the commands meaning what they meant).

### 5.4 Packs (38)

| Kind | Packs (language) |
|---|---|
| Browser apps | `browser-javascript` (JS), `browser-react`, `browser-vue`, `browser-svelte`, `browser-angular` (TS) |
| Node/TS services | `node-http-api` (JS), `node-nest-api`, `node-hono-api`, `node-hono-native-api` (TS) |
| Full stack | `fullstack-node` (JS), `fullstack-react-express`, `fullstack-vue-express`, `fullstack-next` (TS), `fullstack-react-fastapi`, `fullstack-vue-fastapi`, `fullstack-page-fastapi`, `fullstack-django` (Python) |
| Python | `python-http-api`, `python-flask-api`, `python-cli`, `python-library` |
| CLIs and libraries | `node-cli`, `node-library` (JS), `typescript-cli`, `typescript-library`, `go-cli`, `go-library`, `rust-cli`, `rust-library` |
| Other services | `go-http-api`, `go-gin-api`, `java-spring-api`, `csharp-aspnet-api`, `rust-axum-api`, `ruby-sinatra-api`, `ruby-rails-api`, `php-slim-api`, `php-laravel-api` |

Fullstack packs share one shape (`packs/_fullstack.py`): the interface built with Vite into `dist/`, served by the product's own service beside `/api/`, one process on one port, driven by Playwright through the page and `/api/`. Service packs (`packs/_service.py`) are tested from outside with Playwright's `request` fixture or pytest + httpx whatever their language, so QA writes acceptance tests one way and a building stage never runs the service by hand. Web packs start from a shared accessible design token stylesheet (`packs/_design.py`), which also styles plain, unclassed markup on those tokens (PQ.7, LF.1378, `packs/base_elements.css`): the page header and surface, fields with hover, focus, disabled and invalid states, a quiet control by default and a primary one for a form's submit control or a pressed or current one, a list row, a message area (`role=alert`) and an empty-state block (`role=status`), elevation and one transition (none under reduced motion). Element and attribute selectors only; nothing hides content or fixes a width. A brief's line limit leaves out the lines the token stylesheet arrived with (PQ.6, LF.1379, `brief_constraints.without_the_pack_s_own_styles`, read by `integration_checks._measure_sizes`); a value the product changed and every rule it added still count. Support status per pack: section 1.4.

### 5.5 Known errors and the golden example

- **`known_errors`**: a phrase of an error message the stack is known to raise, mapped to its cause. A defect whose text carries the phrase is given the cause (`app/stacks/known_errors.py`). Nothing outside the pack names the stack.
- **`golden_example`**: a small working product in the pack's pinned framework version (shared state reaching a handler, one JSON body read and checked, a 404 and a 400, data in a file under `DATA_DIR`, a unit test calling the service in memory). It is shown to stages that write and fix code as reference and never written into the product (`contract.reference_code`); `scripts/loop/verify_packs.py` compiles it and runs its test. Present on 7 packs (`go-http-api`, `go-gin-api`, `java-spring-api`, `csharp-aspnet-api`, `rust-axum-api`, `ruby-sinatra-api`, `php-slim-api`), where version skew was the measured trap; other packs have none.
- **`release_families`** (LF.1311, `app/stacks/stated_releases.py`): the frameworks a pack pins, each declared by the pack (`ReleaseFamily`): how a brief names it, the registry package leading its line, where each pin is written (package, file, a pattern with a `version` group) and what its files say differently on another line (`ReleaseLine`: files, smoke files, golden example, known errors). Declared today by `java-spring-api` (Spring Boot, the `spring-boot-starter-parent` version; the 4 line has its own starters `spring-boot-starter-webmvc` / `-webmvc-test`, smoke test, Jackson 3 golden store and known errors) and `node-nest-api` (NestJS: `@nestjs/core`, `common`, `platform-express`, `testing` in step; no line of its own, so a 12 brief that does not pass its smoke test falls back). See "The release the brief asks for" in 8.3.
- **Naming standard**: one per language, stated in the specification and every draft's request (`app/stacks/naming.py`, `NAMING_STANDARD`).

### 5.6 `seed.py`, the language table and per-language data

- **`app/plugins/seed.py`**: the only place language- or framework-specific instructions for agents belong. Rows are keyed by lower-case names the stack extractor produces (a language, a framework); the middleware appends the rows matching the run's stack. An edited row in the database is never overwritten by re-seeding. `seed.DEVELOPMENT_SERVERS` lists development-server markers a release reads; `seed.DATA_LAYER_FILES` and `seed.INTERFACE_TEMPLATE_SUFFIXES` name a data-layer tool's files and server-side page templates for `analysis/file_domains.py` (SK.1).
- **`app/stacks/languages.py`** (`LanguageSupport`, 10 rows: javascript, typescript, python, go, rust, java, kotlin, csharp, ruby, php). One row per language answers, once, what every mechanism needs: source `suffixes`, `manifests` and `lockfiles`, `installed` folders (known by name), `installed_beside` and `build_output_beside` (known by a manifest next to them, so `vendor` is the product's own unless `composer.json` is beside it), `build_output_by_name`, `test_files` patterns, `check` (the checker that reports undefined names before tests run: `language_service` for JS/TS, `pyright` for Python, a `compiler_check` checker otherwise), `test_totals` (runners whose printed totals the sandbox counts), `runtime_version` (what `tools/runtimes.py` pins), `docs` (the registry `read_docs` reads a README from), `sandbox_toolchain` and `executables` (which image a command runs in), `import_graph`, `development_server`, `package_cache` (the variable and folder when a toolchain keeps its restored packages under the user's home, so a product started as delivered is pointed at the restored cache), `restores_when_it_starts` (`dotnet run`) and `resolved` (the reader of `analysis/resolved_packages.py` that lists what an install resolved with each package's runtime need, for the toolchain-fit check; a gap for Java and Kotlin), and `tree` (KG.2, LF.1510: the grammar of `tree_sitter_language_pack` each suffix is read with, tree-sitter query patterns for its definitions, imports and declared namespace, and the resolver of `analysis/import_resolvers.py` that turns its imports into project files: `module_path` for Go - the nearest `go.mod`'s module and the package folder -, `declared_package` for Java, Kotlin and C#, `crate_modules` for Rust - `mod`, `use crate::`/`self::`/`super::` and the crate's own name -, `relative_require` for Ruby, `autoload_namespace` for PHP - `composer.json`'s `psr-4` prefixes, then declared namespaces -; Python and JavaScript/TypeScript keep `import_graph`'s family). A field with no answer is a `Gap` carrying the reason, never omitted; a completeness test holds every pack's language to a full row. Derived constants: `INSTALLED`, `NOT_THE_PRODUCT`, `MANIFESTS`, `LOCKFILES`, `TEST_FILE_PATTERNS`, `is_generated`, `image_for_executable`.

**The code graph reads every language of the table** (`analysis/project_graph.py`, `.harness/project-graph.json`; KG.2, LF.1510, always on). Each source file a grammar reads is parsed once while its modification time and size are unchanged (`analysis/parsed_definitions.py`): every definition with its kind, name, first and last line and the definition it sits in (a node's `definitions`, `[kind, name, first, last, enclosing]`), its import statements and its declared package or namespace. A tree with error nodes is still read. Outside Python and JavaScript/TypeScript, whose family imports and definitions are as before, a file's `defines` are the tree's names and its `imports` come from its language's resolver; a namespace a `using` or a `.*` opens whole names only the files of it whose names the importing file uses. Where no grammar loads, the line pattern (`_DEFINES_ELSEWHERE`) gives the definitions and no import is followed, and `who_uses` says so. Everything that reads the graph's imports - a fixer's "Where the failure is handled" (FX.2), `SLICED_MEMORY`, the memory's ranking by importers, the neighbourhoods - now has them in Go, Java, Kotlin, C#, Rust, Ruby and PHP. Measured on a generated 2,030-file, 154,000-line codebase of those languages: 1.0 s cold, 0.25 s after one file changed, 0.03 s unchanged. Unreached-module detection (`reachability.FAMILIES`) and the outline still read Python and JavaScript/TypeScript, and the patterns, only. **Which names a file uses of which file** (KG.3, LF.1513, always on, data only; KG.5-KG.8 read it): the same trees give every name a file uses (`TreeReading.references`: bare, after the object itself, after another receiver), looked up by name among the project's definitions with no type resolution (`analysis/name_references.py`). A bare name the file defines is its own; otherwise the definers counted are the files it sees - those it imports, and those its language lets it use without importing (`TreeReading.sees`: Go's package folder, a Java/Kotlin/C#/PHP declared package or namespace, whatever Ruby's requires load) - and, for a method or anything written inside a type, only where the file names that type. Exactly one left: a node's `uses` {name: file}, the definer's `used_by` {name: [files]}, and per definition (`Type.name`) `calls` [[name, file]] and `called_by` [[file, caller]]; none or several: `ambiguous` {name: [files it may be]}, never stated as fact. Same-package use without an import (Go, Java, C#) is now an edge. On the captured products of nine languages no stated edge is wrong. Measured: 190 files (13,000 lines) give a 0.44 MB graph file (0.13 MB before); 2,052 generated files (144,000 lines) build in 1.3-1.5 s cold (0.94 s before), 0.4 s after one change, and the parse cache holds 14 MB more. `reachability.FAMILIES` is not extended: Spring and ASP.NET load controllers by annotation, which no name reaches, and no Go pack is wired by imports.

**Routes, tests and criteria are part of the code graph** (`analysis/routes_and_tests.py`; KG.4, LF.1514, always on). When the graph is built, each file of the product's code gets its `routes`: `[method, path, line, handler file, handler, first, last, criteria]` for each line declaring a route (`declares_a_route`, which reads a method inside the quotes too, `"GET /notes/{id}"`), the path joined to its container's (a route marker above a definition holding other routes or a pathless marker such as `@PostMapping`), and its handler: the definition the marker stands above (a definition read from its first marker counts), the name a method word calls (`get(list_notes)`, in whichever file defines it), or the name passed after the path; else the line itself. Each acceptance test file's tests (split at their declared names) give its `requests`, matched to the routes as FX.2 matches them, so a route carries the criteria of the tests requesting it and its declaring and handling files are `tested_by` those test files; for a page, each test's selectors by text, label, placeholder, title, test id or a role's name (`selects`) are matched to the product files - markup included - holding exactly that text between quotes or tags (`renders`, at most four files a text). A markup file that renders one is given the project scripts it loads as its `imports`, and a page fixer is named them too: the behaviour that changes what it renders is there. A unit test's `references` are the definitions of the product files it imports that it names, and those files list it in `unit_tests` (by import; KG.3's references by name join in `unit_test_references`). "tested by" in the neighbourhoods, `who_uses` and KG.1's reach records is the union of these and the importing test files; a neighbourhood also says what a file `serves` and its unit tests. Measured on 26 stored workspaces of 14 packs: service route files tested by an acceptance test 0 of 19 before, 19 of 19 after; of 223 stored failing-test messages, localised by route 43 and nothing 180 before, route 49, page 30 and nothing 144 after, 49 with the handler named; build time unchanged (0.01-0.08 s). `loop.sh effects` adds the tool calls each localised fixer made before its first write, by how it was localised (one medium run before KG.4: by route, median 22).

**The code graph's answers where the model already looks** (KG.5-KG.8, LF.1537-LF.1540; group "Code graph answers", each switch off in code and on in the compose file, owner 11-17; shared lookups in `analysis/graph_lookups.py`). None adds a tool. (KG.5, `DEFINITIONS_BEFORE_FILES`, `agents/definitions_before_files.py`, read by `project_memory.render`) Where a fixer's localised files (FX.2) - or a sliced builder's files - together take more than 60% of its memory's budget, each is held as the definitions the request reaches, whole and numbered as in the file: the handler FX.2's section names (`is answered by ... lines a-b`), the definition holding each `path:line` the failure names, each definition named in backquotes or before `(`; then each definition those call (one hop of the graph's `calls`) while the room (60% of the budget) lasts, one in a file outside the set under its caller's file; then an outline of the rest by line (`analysis/outline.py`). Files that fit are held whole as before. (KG.6, `GRAPH_IN_SEARCH_ANSWERS`, `agents/graph_in_search_answers.py`, called by the path middleware `workspace_paths` and by `room_for_reads`) A `grep` whose pattern is one name the project defines is answered first with "The harness's code graph: `name` is defined in `file` lines a-b; used in ..." and then the matches; a `read_file` with a range that opens on a definition (within 3 lines of its start) adds one line naming what calls it; a whole read answered by an outline (`READS_SIZED_TO_THE_ROOM`) adds what uses each definition from other files. (KG.7, `USES_NAMED_AT_THE_WRITE`, `tools/uses_named_at_the_write.py`, a middleware of every role agent inside the transcript) A write (`write_artifact`, `edit_artifact`, `replace_lines`, `write_file`, `edit_file`) that removes or renames a definition, or changes its parameter list, is answered after its own reply with each file the graph (as it was before the write) shows using the old name unambiguously and the lines that still use it now, once per definition a stage; a `uses_named_at_the_write` transcript record keeps it. (KG.8, `REVIEWS_HOLD_THE_CHANGE_AND_ITS_USES`, `agents/reviews_hold_the_change.py`) A security review's changed files past its source budget, and a code review's changed files its memory does not hold past the size gate, are given in the request as each definition holding a changed line (against the sprint's accepted commit; every definition of a file new since), innermost, whole and numbered while 16,000 characters last (the rest by signature and lines), each with the signatures and lines of the definitions using it in other files. A review that fits as today is sent as today. `loop.sh effects` prints one line each: fix stages handed definitions against the others (reads, repeat reads, opening tokens), calls from a stage's first search to its first write, notes at a write followed by a write to a file named, and reviews' reads and opening tokens with and without the section.

**Lines chosen before a reading stage opens** (PF.4, LF.1558, `RANGES_CHOSEN_BEFORE_A_READING_STAGE`, group "Fix and reading speed", off in code and on in the compose file, `agents/ranges_chosen_first.py`, read by `project_memory.render_for_a_stage`). Before the suite's author, a test round or the technical lead opens on a project whose files are past the memory's room (`project_memory.budget_for`), one call with reasoning off (at most 600 output tokens) is given the stage's request and an outline of the code (`analysis/outline.py`, each definition and test with its lines, at most 16,000 characters) and names at most 8 ranges as `file: lines a-b`. A range is kept when its file is in the project, within the file's lines and at most 160 lines long. The memory then holds those lines numbered as in the file, under "the lines chosen for this work before it opened", every other code file only in the outline (read by range), and the documents as before, within the stage's budget. Asked once per (workspace, stage, request); the size gate's look at what a stage is shown never asks; a later sprint's suite writer given only the product's interface, a project within its room, or a call that names nothing leaves the memory as it was. A `ranges_chosen` transcript record keeps the ranges; `loop.sh effects` gives those stages' reads and repeat reads against the other reading stages and the reads of files no range named.
- **Sandbox tables** (`app/tools/sandbox.py`): `TOOLCHAINS` (executables per language, 17 languages incl. swift, dart, elixir, scala, c, cpp), `ROLE_EXECUTABLES` (per-role allowlists keyed `<role>_specialist`), `TOOLCHAIN_TIMEOUT_SCALE`, install grammars per package manager.

### 5.7 Contract helpers: install, versions, start-first, custom stacks

- **Install** (`contract.installed`, `install_if_the_manifests_changed`): reinstall when a manifest changed since the last install; when an installer says a package has no such version, or lacks a feature (Cargo), the failure shown to the fixer carries what the registry actually has (`_the_versions_that_exist`, `_the_features_that_exist`) and the installer's own error lines (`_what_the_installer_said_went_wrong`), never usage text. An untrusted lock file is not relied on (`_a_lock_file_the_installer_cannot_trust`). Installs pass the **dependency gate** (a name the registry lacks or one first published fewer than `DEPENDENCY_MIN_AGE_DAYS` = 14 days ago is refused; `tools/dependency_gate.py`) and the egress check (`tools/install_egress.py`: only package registries and GitHub, plus `INSTALL_ALLOWED_HOSTS`).
- **Compiler help**: `compiler_errors.py` runs the pack's `typecheck` once when the product does not start and adds the errors that name something missing (a name, export, member or module) to the one start defect; `entry_exports.py` raises a defect when a stage drops an export the pack's own files import; `import_extensions.py` / `imports_before_a_run.py` add the `.ts` Node needs to a relative import before the harness measures the product and before a stage's command runs; `production_build.py` runs the declared or manifest build once per product fingerprint.
- **Start-first** (`graph/start_first.py`): at a test round, everything saying why the product does not build or start (start failure, compiler and language-service findings, failed install, a unit run that did not compile) is folded into the one start defect; while it is open the fix round dispatches only it; the others wait until the product starts.
- **Foundation smoke** (`acceptance/foundation_smoke.py`, `graph/foundation_first.py`; LF.1268): at each test round of a product that starts and whose suite did not pass every test it ran, a short smoke compiled from the locked contract (no model) runs against the started product as the contract probes do: the product answers; when the contract's sign-up and sign-in are due, a fresh account is made and signed in; one create of the first due create line with the example bodies, and one read of it back (by id, else the list). Only a clear fault fails a step (no answer, 5xx, 404/405, a fresh account refused at sign-in, the sign-in's token refused); any other answer leaves the smoke unsettled and changes nothing. A failed smoke raises one blocking defect for its first failing step with the request, the answer and the server log, and `start_first.held_back` holds every other open defect while it is open (the tier after "does not start"); a smoke that holds closes it, one not asked or unsettled releases it. Steps of later sprints' criteria are not asked; a product with no HTTP contract or no probe runner is not smoked. Result in `.harness/foundation-smoke.json` and the transcript (`foundation_smoke`).
- **Fix order** (`graph/order_of_fixes.py`): a fix round takes the defects its latest test round raised or raised again (highest `found_in_round`) before those carried over from earlier rounds, each group in the order raised, so the cause the latest measurement names leads; with `FIX_ORDER_CRITERIA_FIRST` on, failing criteria and release findings first within each group. Only the order changes; a reorder is recorded once a round (`fix_order`, counted by `loop.sh effects`; LF.1244).
- **Custom stacks** (`stacks/custom_setup.py`): for a stack no pack covers, a setup stage creates the project with the framework's generator on the closest pack's install and runner configuration (`framework_files`, `proven_code`), declares it in `<project>/stack-setup.json`, and the harness proves it exactly as it proves a pack (install, each smoke test through the declared command, JUnit read). Only a setup that passes is recorded as a contract; one that does not is sent back once. With no runner layer, the stage starts from the harness's own draft of the declaration (PA.11, LF.1381, `custom_setup.draft_declaration`): a pack of the stack's language and kind taken whole, else the unit side from the language's first pack and the acceptance side from the kind's (no pack of its language: the unit side left empty); `kept_files` and the guides are the stage's. Its request, layered or not, states the sandbox's facts (`environment_contract.the_setup_facts`): whether the browser build is already installed (and never to install one or its system libraries), that nothing runs as root, and that a command is one program with its arguments.
- **Release** (`stacks/release.py`, `release_docs.py`, `release_audits.py`): CI generated from the pack's own commands so it cannot describe a build nobody saw pass; container file for services; README with run instructions and an API summary required (`RELEASE_DOCS_REQUIRED`); secret scan, licence listing and an `AUDIT_COMMANDS` dependency audit step in CI (`RELEASE_AUDITS`). No container is ever built inside the sandbox, and the release record says so.

---

## 6. Acceptance suite and verification

### 6.1 Authoring

`app/acceptance/criteria.py` numbers `AC-n` from the brief; `suite.save_criteria` keeps them in `.harness/acceptance.json`. The QA author (testing specialist with `writing_the_suite=True`) is given the criteria, the pack's `acceptance_guide`, the brief's control names up front, the **API reference** (`acceptance/api_reference.py`: the specification's routes, data shapes and error messages handed over whole, within a bound), the interface contract and testability advice. Each test name carries its criterion tag. A criterion QA says cannot be automated is deferred with a reason only if no test covers it. A change request adds `CR-n`. A criterion that compares values in a normal form is tested with values in another form, and a test of a group or count of them checks that the key reported is the normal form (LF.1255, LF.1317).

### 6.2 Mechanical review (before the QA lead reads anything)

`acceptance_review` runs the suite once against the scaffold and applies about fifty named checks (`suite.py`, `check_findings.py` records every finding with the check that raised it so `scripts/loop/check_precision.py` can measure each check's precision). Families:

| Family | Examples |
|---|---|
| Cannot run or proves nothing | `result.ran`, `vacuous_green`, the test that never finishes, files the runner skips by name (renamed, not sent back) |
| Cannot pass on any product | `tests_that_cannot_pass`, the dry run of a JS suite with the product stood in for (`spec_dry_run.cjs`, `SPEC_DRY_RUN`), a test that reads a literal id and creates nothing first (`IDS_READ_WITHOUT_A_CREATE`), `tests_that_want_json_from_a_204`, success statuses the contract does not give; a test whose pick of its own records misses one it made (`picks_of_their_own_records_that_miss_one`, the pack's `own_records_a_pick_misses`, JavaScript; also handed to a dispute as settling, LF.1237); a page test asserting an exact number of every record the page lists while every test shares one service (`counts_of_every_record_on_a_shared_page`, the pack's `counts_of_every_listed_record`, JavaScript: `toHaveCount(n)` or a `.count()` compared with `toBe(n)` on an unnarrowed list; before the lock, in rounds, and settling a dispute, LF.1246) |
| A product failure read as a test's | a `TypeError` reading a field of null that came from the product's answer (a helper returning null on a refused answer) is the product's, not the test's (`_javascript.null_from_the_product`, LF.1241); advice names every test that reads an answer before checking its status (`answers_read_without_their_status`) |
| Release probes sign in | every body probe (server error, bad body, emptied field, concurrent write, wrong shape, edge, record first) takes its example bodies from the specification and the suite's criteria (`bodies_for_the_probes`), the first body carrying a secret always kept, so a product behind a bearer sign-in is probed signed in (LF.1242) |
| Passes before the product exists | `tests_that_pass_before_the_product` (first writing only), assertions made only when the answer was good (`tests_guarded_by_the_answer`) |
| Too lenient | locators nobody sees, locators that pick one of many, a control the brief names that no test finds by that name, records never found as the brief shows them, outputs held only in part, whitespace and word-break tests with spaces only, views never acted within |
| Coverage | every criterion has a test; every contract line is exercised by some test (`contract_coverage`, `CONTRACT_LINE_COVERAGE`, a search not a parse) |

A suite under repair is measured first (`_the_repair_measured`): a repair that left the suite worse is put back as locked and the writers are told what it lost; tests a repair changed that were not sent for repair go back as the review approved them.

**A limit on calling, reconciled before the lock (LF.1269, always on).** Before the suite is measured at each review, `acceptance/client_limits.py` reads, with no model, each limit the specification's security, reliability and API sections set on how often one client may call (a count in a window, per address or per user, and the operation it limits; prose that only mentions a rate, lockouts and operations the suite's addresses do not match are not read). The suite's language reader (`_javascript.client_calls`, `_python.client_calls`) counts the requests each test makes to that route, through helpers, hooks, fixtures and literal-bound loops. When the suite calls past the limit within its window, the one finding is the specification's, never the writers': a harness ruling (`specification_rulings.the_harness_rules`, shown to every later stage with the other rulings whatever `SETTLE_SPEC_CONTRADICTIONS` says) and a decision - the limit is declined for this release when the brief does not ask for one, else its count is read from `CLIENT_RATE_LIMIT`. Every test command then gets `CLIENT_RATE_LIMIT` (`.harness/client_limits.json`, at least 1000), which the pack's start for the suite (its Playwright config or conftest) passes on to the product. A specification with no such limit, or a suite within it, is unchanged.

### 6.3 The QA lead and the lock

When the harness's checks are clear (or at the limit with problems open and no review yet), the QA lead reviews (`agents/acceptance_reviewer.py`): would each test fail if its criterion were unmet, are the QA-deferred criteria's reasons valid. Findings about the product rather than the tests are dropped (`findings_about_the_suite`). Then `suite.lock`: files copied to `.harness/acceptance-snapshot/` with checksums, recorded as `acceptance-locks/vN` with a diff from the previous version quoted by the decision that re-locks it. At lock the interface contract is recorded (`interface_contract.record_at_lock`), contract line coverage recorded, and the contract's own probes run once (`probe_at_the_lock`). Before that, when the specification is written, each contract line's criterion id is checked against the numbered criteria (`acceptance/contract_ids.py`, always on, LF.1475): a line whose named criterion shares no word with its route, control name or command (or its exit status) is given the one other criterion that states it (its route, control or command, else its words); failing that, when the ids run 1, 2, 3 ... down the lines, the id is taken off and the line is checked charging no criterion (`interface_contract.parse` reads a page or command line with none). Measured: 7 of 15 runs numbered the lines by line. The `acceptance_tests` decision states what the lock ran and any problem left open.

**Due tests only, during a sprint (LF.1265, always on).** While a sprint is built, every run of the suite (the test round, a fixer's before/after measurement, a repair's review) takes only the **due** tests - those whose names carry a criterion due now (the sprint's own and every earlier sprint's) - with the earliest sprint's files first where the runner keeps the order it is given. The workflow records the scope from the state at each sprint's start and before each test round (`acceptance_nodes.record_the_sprint_scope` -> `.harness/sprint-scope.json`, now with the plan's criteria by sprint); `acceptance/due_tests.py` decides, and the pack asks its runner through `StackPack.acceptance_selection`: Playwright packs `--grep /<ids>/i` (files in Playwright's own order), Python packs `-k` on `ac3_`-style names with the due files listed in sprint order, Go programs `-run`. A pack without the hook (Node's own runner, Rust, a custom setup) runs the whole suite as before. Either way a test whose every id is a later sprint's is dropped from the report, and a criterion a later sprint delivers reads `not_yet_due` - never failing, untested or deferred - so it is neither counted nor shown to a fixer or the lead. A run whose filter left out a due criterion's test runs the whole suite instead. The last sprint and the release (`sprint_scope.record_everything_due`, whatever sprint the run left from) take every test; a product planned as one sprint runs the pack's command unchanged.

**Written and locked a sprint at a time (LF.1266, owner 2026-11-24, always on).** The criteria and the interface contract are fixed up front; what changes is when each sprint's tests are written. At the first writing `acceptance_author` numbers the criteria and asks `by_sprint.decide`: a product whose criteria allow more than one sprint (`max_sprints_for_criteria_count` > 1: thorough delivery always, fast delivery past 20 criteria) is recorded as written by sprint (`written_by_sprint` in `.harness/acceptance.json`) and goes to `scrum_master` before any test exists; the planner is given the numbered criteria. Any other product, and a run whose suite was already locked whole, keeps the old order exactly (suite, review, lock, then plan). The plan (and the plan gate's go) goes to `sprint_acceptance`, as does `sprint_advance`: the scope is recorded first, the due criteria no test names are opened as an **addition** (`by_sprint.open_for`: its criteria and the checksums of what is locked), and QA writes only those, told which sprint's part it writes and that the earlier sprints' files stay as locked (new files beside them; their helpers imported). A later sprint's writers are told the earlier sprints' product is running and that the product's code never decides what a test expects (the suite dispute's own rule, `suite_dispute.THE_PRODUCT_S_CODE_IS_NOT_THE_STANDARD`). They are handed, read by the harness, the product's interface - the routes its code declares or mounts with their handlers' signatures, the shapes those handlers take and give, and the earlier locked tests' helpers and fixtures by signature - and their project memory leaves the product's code and unit tests out (LF.1362, `acceptance/product_interface.py`, `project_memory.written_from_the_interface`). The lock only adds: during an addition the write tools refuse QA a file locked with an earlier sprint (`repository._locked_with_an_earlier_sprint`), the review puts back any such file that changed (and says so), drops findings about earlier sprints' locked tests alone and about later sprints' criteria alone, gives the QA lead only the new files (the earlier ones by name), and checks brief-wide promises (controls, records, views, whole outputs) and contract-line coverage only once nothing is not yet due. An earlier sprint's tests still change through the repair and dispute paths. A later addition that cannot run or proves nothing at the round limit is left out, its criteria deferred, and the run goes on (a first writing still fails the run). Each lock appends to the open problems the earlier ones left. A re-plan needs nothing of its own: "not written" is read from the suite (no test names the criterion), so a criterion moved to a later sprint keeps its locked tests, which run when that sprint comes, and one moved to a sprint that has begun is written at the next `sprint_acceptance`. Readers treat a criterion whose sprint has not started as not yet due: `suite.gaps`, `_due_criteria_with_no_test`, the review's checks, the release. The escalation's foundation check (`foundation.later_work_needs`) finds no shared-step evidence in unwritten later criteria and rests on the plan's dependencies and the product's start. Module `acceptance/by_sprint.py`.

### 6.4 Probes against the started product

| Probe | When | What it asserts | Module |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Start probe** | when the suite cannot reach the product (`START_PROBE`, built in) | starts the product as the suite's configuration does (its `start.sh`, a free `PORT`, a fresh empty `DATA_DIR`), polls the readiness URL, reports what the service printed and the URL answered, with the lines stating the error ahead of the stack frames (`what_went_wrong`); in container mode the pack's `list_routes` supplies routes when the readiness route is not served | `acceptance/start_probe.py/.cjs`, `start_output.py` |
| **Interface-contract probes** | at lock, each green round, release | the contract is one strict line per route, control or command (`AC-3 GET /api/notes -> 200`, `AC-1 page /login textbox "Email"`, `AC-5 command notes add milk -> exit 0`), compiled without a model so a repair of the suite cannot loosen them: a bare request must not draw a 5xx, an unparsable body must be refused, a field the criteria refuse empty is refused, non-numeric ids and a record made first where the route has a parameter | `acceptance/interface_contract.py` |
| **Page checks** | before every suite run for page products | loads the page once, records uncaught console errors, axe-core serious and critical rules (accessibility), width at 375 px; defects for a page whose only styling is the scaffold's reset (`analysis/interface_checks.py`) | pack `page_check`, `runtime_report` |
| **Page probe** | green round (`PAGE_PROBE`) | a Playwright spec through the suite's own configuration drives a person adding an item: markup typed into a field must not run as code, no console errors or failed requests, no sideways scroll at 360 px, an item survives a reload where the brief says data persists; each run's outcome (`ran clean`, `found N`, or `could not run` and why) and each check's result or skip reason are kept as `page` in `.harness/contract-probe.json`, which `loop.sh effects` prints (LF.1437) | `acceptance/page_probe.py` |
| **Edge probe** | green round (`EDGE_PROBE`) | requests compiled from the contract's routes and example bodies: each field missing, null, empty, spaces, wrong type, very long, other script; ids non-numeric, negative, huge, deleted; delete twice. Invariants: never 5xx, a refused create stores nothing, a refused update changes nothing | `acceptance/edge_probe.py` |
| **Round-trip probe** | green round and release (built in, no switch) | a record made through the create line, deleted, then deleted again answers the contract's not-found status (404/410), and its `GET` no longer reads it; each free-text field of the create's example body sent a long text of many lines (about 20,000 characters) is read back exactly; a value accepted and read back otherwise, or a read that fails, is a finding, and so is a refusal (a 4xx but 401/403/413/429) of a field neither the brief nor its criteria hold to a most length (`interface_contract.fields_held_to_a_ceiling`: "at most N characters", "max N", "up to N characters", "maxLength"; the brief kept in `.harness/acceptance.json` as the kept-when-empty probe keeps it) - a limit only the specification adds is what it finds (LF.1298); with no brief known a refusal is not judged. Coverage in `.harness/contract-probe.json` as `round_trip` | `acceptance/round_trip_probe.py` |
| **Kept-when-empty probe** | green round and release (built in, no switch; LF.1273) | the emptied-field probe's twin: when an example body carries a field the brief or its criteria refuse empty, each create and replace write is sent that body with each other free-text field set to `""` (not one any rule of the brief, criteria or specification holds to a floor - not empty, a least length - nor an id, credential, address, date or fixed value); it must be taken (2xx) and answered back as `""`. The plain body refused skips the test. Its defect (`A write refuses a field the brief keeps when empty:`) names the write, the status and the fields the brief refuses, is settled by asking again (`still_raised`, the brief kept in `.harness/acceptance.json`) and still blocks at release; coverage as `kept_empty`. The backend role is told the same: "required" means present, not non-empty, and a length limit the brief does not set is not added (LF.1298) | `acceptance/interface_contract.py` (`kept_empty_checks`, `kept_empty_errors`) |
| **Concurrent-write probe** | green round (`CONCURRENT_WRITE_PROBE`) | N creates at once each kept with its own id | `concurrent_write_probe.py` |
| **Wrong-shape body probe** | green round (`WRONG_SHAPE_BODY_PROBE`) | valid JSON that is not an object must not make the server fail | `wrong_shape_body_probe.py` |
| **Order probe** | first green round | each file run alone, last first; a test failing only then is recorded | `order_probe.py` |
| **Release re-probe** | release | each probe that applies is asked again; what it still finds is a gap that blocks, in the words the round's defect used | `probes_at_release.py` |
| **Screenshot rubric** | release, only when the model reads images (`SCREENSHOT_RUBRIC`; the judgement model, else the run's own, LF.1430) | a fixed checklist scored 1-5, a score never a gate | `screenshot_rubric.py` |

Supporting: `measured_once.py` (an unchanged product is not measured again), `unstable.py` (a criterion failing in the whole suite and passing alone on the same fingerprint is unstable, not fixed), `per_file.py` (a suite that hangs is run file by file with a short limit), `stray_services.py` (services a test started and left running are stopped after the command), `unmeasured.py` (a measurement the harness could not take, such as the sandbox refusing for memory or an unreachable registry, is not a product failure and charges no round), `sprint_scope.py` (which locked tests belong to later sprints, so a builder is not made to pass them early), `due_tests.py` (which tests a sprint's run takes, §6.3).

### 6.5 Mutation testing

`acceptance/mutation.py`: small textual changes to the product's source (a comparison turned round, a condition flipped, a constant moved by one), the suite run as the harness runs it, and each change no test notices reported. Each run of the tests with a change in place is held to three times the unchanged run plus 30 s (at least 60 s, `sandbox.capped`); tests that never return count as having noticed the change. With `MUTATION_GAPS_BUNDLED` the findings in one file go to one fixer and each is closed only when `mutation_guided.settled` confirms it. It runs at release when criteria pass, for a suite that drives no browser, about three changes. With `MUTATION_GUIDED_TESTS` (bundle `PREMIUM_DEEP_VERIFICATION`) the same probe runs when a sprint's due criteria all pass, on the sprint's changed source files: a surviving change becomes a finding for the testing role asking for a unit test that fails with the change and passes without it (`mutation_guided.py`). `MUTATION_TEST_RETENTION` (built in) keeps what a fix added only if it earns its place at file granularity. `MUTANT_CHECK_IN_THE_FIX_STAGE` tells the fixer at each write whether its test notices the change; `EQUIVALENT_MUTANTS_REMEMBERED` records a change judged equivalent so it is not raised again (`mutation_equivalents.py`, `.harness/equivalent_mutants.json`). Mutation findings the sprint has no round for are gaps, not a reason to re-plan. `scripts/bench/mutate.py` measures a run's locked suite the same way for the benchmark.

With `CONTESTED_TESTS_CONFIRMED` (off until measured), rulings that quote identical code at fault count as one cause for the cap, and a ruling past the cap reopens a test only after the harness confirms its quoted repair: no line that checks a result changed, the locked test fails alone, a corrected copy beside it passes, and that copy still fails against at least one of up to 3 deliberate changes to the product (`graph/contested_tests.py`).

A finding of the suite's dry run carries the cause when the runner's error is a known one (`spec_dry_run.SUITE_KNOWN_ERRORS`), and is never held back, since it is what running the test's own code did (LF.1183). Any other suite-check finding that comes back with the same check, wording and tests after a repair changed the suite is not sent to the authors again: it goes to the QA lead as a suspected false finding and is recorded (`suspected false finding`, a `withheld` row in `check-findings.jsonl`; LF.1177). The suite writer's system prompt names only its acceptance folder (LF.1178).

A defect holding a release probe's finding leads any merge with failing unit tests and never counts as a unit-test failure, so a green sprint does not settle while a probe still finds what would block release (LF.1186). A 5xx from the edge or server-error probe reaches its fixer with what the server printed for that request (LF.1185; an HTML error page is cut to its title).

A repair of a locked suite keeps one record (`.harness/suite-repair.json`, `acceptance/suite_repair.py`): the tests sent, their code at the start, every measured version and the best. A review that measures the suite worse than the better of its state before the repair and the best version, or a repair that reaches its round limit, puts back the best version; the lock never takes a worse one (LF.1189-1190). The record also keeps what sent each test - the harness's checks or a judgement (a dispute, a reviewer): a test sent on the checks alone and left as it was is settled, not sent back, once no check that can reopen a suite names it on a measurement of the started product (LF.1267).

### 6.6 Brownfield: the regression contract

For every seeded run the regression baseline is built in (`REGRESSION_CONTRACT` in `BUILT_IN`), and intake, stack selection, the architect and the lead read a code map of the seeded codebase (`agents/code_map.py`, no model call: every module with its public names up to 80 source files, by directory past that, at most 6,000 characters). Its entry points, test reach and main modules come from the full code graph in every language (KG.9, LF.1534, always on): a file defining a function `main` is an entry point, and so is a file no other source file imports or uses a name of, where the graph follows that language's imports (a Go router its package's `main` calls is not one); the modules that define names the request uses (the brief, the specification: a name whose every word of three letters or more is a word of the request) are listed with the files using those names and the tests reaching them; past 80 files a list of main modules comes first, and each directory names its main modules, ranked by a walk over the project's uses (PageRank, as Aider ranks its map: each edge weighed by its names, a name the request uses ten times, the walk restarting at the modules defining them ten times more). Before it a seeded Go or Java codebase had no entry point and its directories ranked modules by importers, which those languages showed none of. With `PULL_REQUEST_DELIVERY` on, the changed product goes back as one commit on the seed's base, pushed as `harness/<slug>-<id8>` with a pull request opened through the host's API (GitHub first, `server/repository_delivery.py`); without a delivery token, or when the push fails, the handover pack carries a git bundle and a patch. The per-project delivery token is sealed with the stored-credentials key, reaches git only through `GIT_ASKPASS`, and is deleted once the pull request is open.

With `REGRESSION_CONTRACT`, a seeded codebase's own green tests and the locked tests that already pass form a baseline taken when the suite is locked; CI raises any that later fail as regressions (`acceptance/regression_contract.py`).

### 6.7 The dispute and reclassification path

1. **Mechanical first.** Tests that cannot pass are named by the checks above and reopened for QA by `reopen_the_suite_for` (the budget: 2 repairs, 4 counting tests never sent before; a test is "sent" once).
2. **The QA lead's dispute** (`acceptance_dispute`) runs when a failed attempt has not yet been judged (`_a_dispute_is_due`, before the re-plan), or mid-attempt when a fix stage left its defect open and blames the locked suite, or two fixes in a row were reverted for breaking the same locked test, or - before a criterion's fixer is sent - its tests run on their own include one no fix in this sprint can make pass: 404 or 405 at a route only a later sprint's criteria name, or 429 under a limit the specification sets (`first_failure.held_as_a_dispute`, LF.1413; the round comes back to the defect after it, and its fixer is told those tests are not its own). The lead is shown each failing locked test with code, criterion, failure and the page it failed on and names only those no product meeting the criterion could pass (`agents/suite_dispute.py`; verdict structured, `STRUCTURED_DISPUTE_VERDICT`).
3. **The harness checks the ruling** before acting: it re-measures the named test alone and with its service (a test that passes alone, or whose service stayed up, is not "wrong"), requires the ruling to quote the test's own criterion (`DISPUTE_QUOTES_THE_CRITERION`) and a standard the specification, brief or contract holds, groups rulings by shared cause (one broken helper is one cause), and applies the **reclassification cap**: `max(2, locked tests // 10)` causes per run (`reclassification_room`). The outcome is one decision plus a line in `.harness/dispute-outcomes.jsonl`.
4. **QA repairs** the named tests only as far as their own code is wrong. A repair is re-measured and judged by the harness's checks; one that changed only the tests it was sent for is re-locked without another QA lead review (`TARGETED_SUITE_REPAIR`).
5. **QA says the product is at fault.** A repair a dispute opened may answer "the test is right, the failure is the product's" (`QA_SAYS_THE_PRODUCT_IS_AT_FAULT`, `graph/product_at_fault.py`); it stands only when the reopen was a dispute's, every sent test is claimed for the first time, the suite is byte for byte its last lock, the harness's checks find nothing, each claimed test fails in the review's own measurement and each claim quotes the standard and the measured failure word for word. Then the suite stays locked, nothing is charged to the repair budget.
6. After the repair the suite is locked again and the sprint goes back to where it was found: the round, or the lead (`acceptance_return_to`); a repaired suite that passes in full beside passing unit tests (or is near green, `NEAR_GREEN_REPAIR_TO_ROUND`) is measured again, not planned again.
7. A test whose repairs are spent and that still cannot pass is **beyond repair**: its criterion is deferred and named, and the release lists reclassified tests as gaps (`suite_reclassified_tests`).

### 6.8 Hidden and quality tests (scoring)

`benchmarks/<family>/` holds `brief.md`, `bench.json` (kind, runner, test count), `hidden/` (never copied into a workspace or the image; the image copies only `src/`), `quality/`, and `reference/` (an implementation that must score 100% to prove the tests right). Families span browser apps, HTTP services in many languages (the `notes-api-*` set share one hidden suite), CLIs, libraries, full-stack pages, an accounts-and-per-user-data service (`tasks-api`) and a brownfield change (`tasks-api-change`: a run starts from the reference base and adds features without breaking the old ten tests). `scripts/bench/score.py` starts the product the way its brief says, runs the hidden and quality tests, computes the handoff score, records the harness's own verdict and marks false acceptances; `scripts/bench/report.py` totals them. Hidden briefs state an Interface section so any implementation can pass.

Beside the hidden score, a **product quality score** (PQ.10, LF.1341, `analysis/product_quality.py`, `loop.sh quality`;
`score` records it in `data/quality/<run>.json`) reads the stored product and its specification with no model call:
interface (a stylesheet loaded, every stylesheet loaded, no rule styling a class or id nothing uses, the contrast of
each declared text/background pair in every colour scheme the stylesheet declares, no raw colour where colour tokens
exist, every field labelled, words for an empty list, a request in flight and a failure, no focus outline removed
without a replacement), data (the domain model's fields declared per record, the stated id kind, unique fields declared
unique, an index and migrations when the specification asks) and service (one error shape and the specification's,
no 500 near a client's fault, a refusal the code raises answered 403, and no product file importing a package its
nearest manifest declares for development only - per language through the language table's `development_only` reader,
`analysis/development_packages.py`: npm's `devDependencies` not also run-time, Python's development groups and
`requirements-dev.txt`; in a package bundled for a page only the files its scripts start; files the stack pack keeps
are not the product's, LF.1390). Counted, not scored: comment lines in product code in blocks that think aloud
("Let's assume...", "Spec says...", two such lines make a block count, LF.1391). Each part is the share of its
applicable checks passed; the overall score is their mean. It gates nothing.

---

## 7. Fix machinery

### 7.1 The defect ledger and cause keys

Every defect is a record with severity, owner (one of the six specialist roles; spellings like "Testing Specialist" are normalised, `agents/specialist_roles.py`), reproduction, expected and actual behaviour, affected files, `fix_attempts`, `attempts` (per attempt: stage, outcome, files, calls), `caught_by`, `kind`, `evidence`, `cause_key`.
