Statuses: `open`, `fixed`, `accepted_risk`, `superseded` (closed by a defect raised for the one cause behind several). `app/sprint/cause.py` derives the cause key deterministically from the measurement (a failing test, a compiler error, a probe's request, a start's error line) so two criteria raised from one template are not mistaken for one recurring defect. `observability/defect_ledger.py` writes each event to the transcript.

Failing acceptance tests are read for their answer and first failing step (`acceptance/first_failing_step.py`): the status an assertion got (a status outside a `toContain` list too, LF.1489), or the status or rate limit an error thrown on purpose names (a shared helper's "Login rate limited" counts as 429), and the frame several tests throw at, else the request's method and leading resource (a 404's step is the resource alone, any method). One refusal behind nearly every failure is one blocking defect (`one_refusal_for_all`); besides it, or without it, at least 3 tests of 2+ criteria and a tenth of the failures stopping at one step with one answer are one blocking defect each, raised ahead of the per-criterion ones, whose criteria are not raised again that round (`a_step_many_tests_stop_at`; a 404 under one prefix carries the hint to compare served routes with called paths). Different statuses or steps are never merged.

### 7.2 Grouping and bundling

Defects that share a cause are bundled so one fixer sees all of them (`_bundle_co_defects`; the fixer is told "the harness measures each one after your fix"), and in parallel mode `_defects_to_fix_together` picks as many as can be fixed side by side: files apart, bundles apart, at most `concurrent_requests()`. A file rewritten for one defect is also shown the other open defects that point at it (`CO_DEFECT_FAILURES_IN_REWRITES`), and a defect another fix already resolved is closed before it is dispatched (`_closed_if_an_earlier_fix_resolved_it`). One start defect holds back all others (5.7). A defect whose own tests pass when re-measured alone at dispatch closes (unless the measurement is the unstable kind, 6.4).

### 7.3 Which fix path a defect takes

`nodes/fixes._fix_one` (per defect):

1. Skip if an earlier fix closed it, or the defect has spent `most_calls()` = 60 model calls across attempts, or one more attempt at the average of its earlier ones would pass that (`defect_budget.spent_out`, left open with its attempts as diagnosis; LF.1434). The calls are counted by the cycle's attempt records, the run's ledger by defect id, or the fix journal by problem key (`cause_journal.calls_on_the_problem`: each journal entry keeps its attempt's calls), whichever has counted the most, so a re-plan or a change of failing tests does not start the count again.
2. Pick the model (`the_build_model`, reasoning on for a fix; judgement after 2 failed fixes or a revert).
3. Failing unit tests are grouped by their first stated error with the values it compared (`Expected`/`Received`/`Actual`, `left`/`right`, `got`/`want`), a bare `Failed` or the test's own name read past (`ci._its_cause`, LF.1451). An edit refused because the file changed names the writing stage only when its record is not older than the file; otherwise the change is not blamed on a stage (`project_memory.who_changed`, LF.1450). `defect_fix_modes.begin` fixes what the stage may write (`TESTS_ONLY_WRITES`, `MUTATION_TEST_RETENTION`). A failing unit test whose stand-in hides a rule a passing acceptance test shows the product keeps is named to its fixer (`analysis/stand_in_tests.py`, LF.1312). Failing unit tests that count or list records other tests of their file stored are named to their fixer with the records that leaked (`analysis/shared_store_tests.py`, LF.1316).
4. Before any new call, **replay kept candidates**: a fix reverted for breaking tests whose own defect's tests passed was kept as a patch (`kept_fix_candidates`, `KEPT_FIX_CANDIDATES`); the smallest diff first, composed across attempts when files do not overlap, measured as any fix is.
5. **Diagnose** a cause that has already failed twice with one call that only diagnoses, on the judgement model or else the run's own (`context.a_model_to_call`, LF.1430); the hypothesis is kept in the journal and shown to the fixer (`cause_journal.diagnosed`, `DIAGNOSE_AFTER` = 2, `DIAGNOSE_A_RECURRING_CAUSE`).
6. **Rewrite-based fix** (`graph/rewritten_fixes.py`, used by default in fast delivery and after a failed fix in every mode; `bounded_fixes.py`): the harness gathers what failed (the defect's own failing tests first, the failure text with traceback frames outside the project collapsed, `analysis/tracebacks.py`), the files it names and the files the failure points at - and where none of those is the product's code, the files the code graph localises the failure to (the handler of the route a failing test requests, a trace's frames, a page's renderer; recorded as a `fixer_localised` record, LF.1528) - the specification's entries for each, and neighbouring files; one call per file asks for the file whole; the compiler's diagnostics on what was written are fed back for up to `MOST_PASSES` = 3 passes and the version with the fewest findings is kept (`graph/fewest_findings.py`). Files too long to return whole are fixed by edits anchored on their own text (`anchored_edits.py`). `LOCALISE_FIRST` asks once, cheaply and without reasoning, which files and symbols (`graph/localisation.py`). After a failed fix, `CANDIDATES` = 3 candidate rewrites are asked of the judgement model (or the fix's own model with the fix cycle on; in a local run the model the stage builds, LF.1430) and the first the defect's tests pass is kept; a rewrite prompt byte-identical to three that already failed is not sent again, and a reply that writes the same files a rewrite or candidate already wrote and that were measured failing is not written or measured again - the defect goes to the agent loop (`REPEATED_REWRITE_PROMPT_STOP`, built in; the reply's files are fingerprinted per workspace, a rewrite counted when written, a candidate when its tests fail; LF.1435). **After a revert** (LF.1547, always on, `graph/what_a_revert_broke.py`): while a fix stage's reverted change is still in the workspace, the fix guard's `on_revert` keeps what it broke - each broken test (at most 3) with what its runner said of it (the pack's unit report, the suite's saved result; bounded as the unit run's own record is), and its change as a diff against where it started (at most 2,400 characters, the files its failures name first) - in `.harness/reverted-fixes.json`, by the defect's cause key or its wording, so a re-planned sprint's defect reads it too. Every later fixer of that defect, the one-file rewrite (`fix_request`) and the agent loop alike, is shown the last two such reverts under "What earlier changes for this defect broke" with the instruction to keep those tests passing. A defect whose last two one-shot rewrites (the rewrite, a candidate, or the rewrite after a surrender; not a replayed candidate) were reverted for breaking the same test is not rewritten a third time: the agent loop takes it with both failures in front of it, since it can run and read the test and change one that is out of date, which the rewrite of the product's files cannot (ledger `rewrite skipped`; the forward repair is not a route here - it runs at the measurement, where it applies). A schema-conformance finding whose fix was reverted for breaking a test that passed is doubtful: it is recorded in the check findings (`schema conformance, contradicted by a passing test`), noted in the ledger as a `suspected false finding`, accepted as a risk with that reason, and the CI gate does not raise it again (`doubtful`, read before the gate's cap of 4, so it takes no place among the findings raised). Counted by `loop.sh effects` (rewrites told, repeats stopped, findings set aside). Measured before it: one schema finding (a field read from the words "generated on successful signin") was rewritten alone three times, each rewrite breaking the same unit test, until it went to a person; its later requests named the test only. **A stuck defect's candidates** (PF.2, LF.1556, `CANDIDATE_FIXES_WHEN_STUCK`, group "Fix and reading speed", off in code and on in the compose file, `graph/candidate_fixes_when_stuck.py`): from a defect's second failed attempt the candidates are as many as the model server has room for now - its slots (`workflow.concurrent_requests`) less the model calls in flight on it (`call_gate.in_flight`), one call per file a candidate, at most 3; fewer than 2 fit and they are asked as before - each with its own seed on a self-hosted OpenAI-compatible server (none sent to a hosted API). A candidate whose own tests pass is also measured against the regression set (the whole suite, `ci.take`, against the last measurement; a product that no longer starts counts) before it is kept, its files put back between tries; the first that passes both answers the attempt, and one that broke a test that passed is kept for the defect's next attempt as a reverted fix is (`kept_fix_candidates.keep_from_the_tree`, the workspace's diff against its last commit). One `candidate_fixes` transcript record per such attempt says how many were asked and how each was judged; `loop.sh effects` counts them.
7. **Agent loop** (the specialist with tools) for a defect with no file the harness can name, a mutation finding (the product's source stays as it is), or a rewrite that wrote nothing. It is capped at `FIX_STAGE_MODEL_CALLS` = 16 and not restarted (`AGENT_LOOP_FIX_LIMITS`); the same action repeated with no file change in between is called a loop and the next input says what it already did. Its last two calls with no answer written keep only the tools that change a file (the first says its next call must be the write), a write sent on the last call is run, and the call after it has only the answer left (LF.1270; the same for every stage offered a tool that changes a file - builders, suite writers, test rounds, setup - since LF.1519, after 18 last writes refused so in 15 visits over 26 runs; a stage that only looks has only its answer there). Each successful write is followed by the defect's own tests and one line of the result is appended to the write's reply (`verify_after_write`, `VERIFY_AFTER_WRITE`). With `TESTS_REACHED_FIRST` on (PF.3, LF.1557, group "Fix and reading speed", off in code and on in the compose file, `graph/tests_reached_first.py`), that check first runs the tests the code graph says reach the files changed since the fix began (KG.4: the acceptance files of the criteria those files are tested by, other than the defect's own, with a test that passed at the last measurement, run file by file as the suite runs them; the unit run when a unit test reaches them and the unit tests passed before): a test that passed before and fails now is named in the write's reply with the end of its message, the defect's own tests are not run, and the check counts as not passed; when they pass the defect's own tests run as before. A `tests_reached_first` record per check that ran them gives its seconds, for `loop.sh effects`. An answer that changed no file is replaced by the rewrite path once (`surrender_to_rewrite`). That rewrite takes, after the defect's own evidence and under the same rules, the files the fixer wrote, tried to write (a refused edit too) or named in its answer (`surrender_evidence.aimed_at`, LF.1401); a path printed under the sandbox's mount (`/workspace/...`) is read as the workspace's (LF.1402). A rewrite that finds no file records which rule left out which file (a test of a criterion the acceptance suite judges, an attempt held to tests only, the locked suite, a scratch file, no such file), and one that writes nothing records what each file's rewrite came to (came back unchanged, an edit that matched nowhere, refused by the language service) (LF.1402-LF.1403). A fixer of a batch fixed side by side gets the same rewrite when it changed nothing, on the files no other fixer of its batch holds or wrote (`batch_file_claims.held_by_another`), and the files the rewrite writes are that fixer's when the batch is measured; a fixer that gave no answer at all (the harness's record of spent calls or a reply cut off) is a surrender too, and the files it read most (`MOST_READ_TAKEN` = 3) join what it aimed at after those it tried to write (`surrender_evidence.gave_no_answer`, LF.1404).
8. **Measure and keep or revert** (`engineering.after_a_fix`): the whole suite and unit tests are measured; anything that passed and now fails reverts the stage to the commit it started from and the defect stays open with what the attempt broke. Exceptions: a fix that gained at least `MIN_GAINED` (3; 4 if it also broke a locked criterion) tests and `RATIO` times as many as it broke, and left the product building and starting, is kept (`net_gain.py`, `KEEP_NET_GAINS`); a fix that makes a locked test pass and breaks only unit tests is kept and each is raised (`unit_tests_disagree.py`); a manifest left unreadable, an install the fix broke, or errors a fix added to the compiler are reasons to revert too (`what_the_fix_made_certain_to_fail`, `what_the_fix_made_the_compiler_report`, `what_the_fix_did_to_the_install`). When a split of the fix (its test changes kept, its product changes put back) breaks too, the fix is put back as written before anything else judges it. A fix the net-gain rule keeps is not split first (LF.1447); a split is recorded (`.harness/put-back.json`, charged to the batch's fixers whose files were put back) and the fixer's attempt is recorded `reverted: its change to ... was put back and its tests kept, because it broke <tests>` with a `reverted` ledger event, so the next fixer reads which tests.
   **A batch fixed side by side is measured once** (`engineering.after_a_batch`): when it breaks what passed before it, each fixer's written files (`batch_file_claims.written_by`), newest writer first, are put back in turn and the rest measured; the first whose putting back leaves nothing broken is the culprit and only its change is reverted (A3, at most `MOST_ATTRIBUTION_MEASUREMENTS` = 3 measurements). Only a fixer that wrote is a suspect (LF.1543, `graph/batch_attribution.py`, always on): one whose writes were all refused, or that changed nothing, is never reverted with the batch nor sent to be fixed alone, and when one fixer wrote every product file the batch changed it is the culprit with no further measurement (the batch without it is the state measured before). With no fixer's writes recorded, or no one fixer to blame, the whole batch is put back. Each broken batch leaves a `batch_measured` transcript record, counted by `loop.sh effects` as "batch reverts by cause".
   **Repaired forward before a revert** (LF.1271, `graph/judged_fixes.py`): a fix about to be reverted because the product no longer starts or builds (it did before the fix), because the unit tests no longer build, or because every test it broke - all acceptance tests that passed before it - now stops at one and the same step (the same shared frame or request, the same answer; `judged_fixes.the_new_step`, read with `first_failing_step`) is handed back once: the start error or that step with the first failure, the end of the server's log, and its own diff against where it started, to the fix's own model, which may rewrite only the files the fix changed (the rewrite path, so its own bounds: `MOST_FILES` = 4 files, `MOST_PASSES` = 3 passes; synchronous, so only in a run that approves its own writes, never for a mutation finding). The fix is then measured again and judged as any fix is: anything that passed before and still fails reverts it as before. One repair a fix stage; a regression is never kept unrepaired. Recorded as the ledger event `repaired forward` (`repaired forward and kept, measured again: ...` or `reverted after a forward repair failed: ...`, counted by `loop.sh effects`) and in the attempt's outcome. A batch is not repaired: its fixers put back are fixed alone next, where the repair applies.
9. **Best checkpoint** (`graph/best_checkpoint.py`): a fix alone commits each write whose immediate check finds the defect's own tests passing, beside the branch. When the stage ends worse than that checkpoint, the checkpoint is put back and measured in the stage's place, kept only if that measurement holds. Not used by fixers of a batch (they share a workspace).
10. **Settle** (`workflow._settle_the_fix`, LF.1260): a kept fix closes its defect only on a measurement - its own tests in the snapshot just taken, or a harness check showing it gone (the language service, the interface check, a regressed test passing, the start, a page check). A defect with no criterion is settled by unit tests only when it was raised from them (the unit-tests-fail heading, or failing-test evidence naming unit tests), never by unit tests that merely mention its files (LF.1443). With none it stays open as **unverified** (ledger event `unverified`, attempt outcome `unverified: ...`, KPI `defects_unverified`; not counted as a failed fix), and a test round that finds nothing wrong closes it (`sprint.closed_by_a_round_that_found_nothing`). A stage after which no file of the product differs from its start (`ci.unchanged_since`, which leaves out the workflow's documents, `.harness/`, test reports, caches and the `tmp-inline-*` file a command line was saved to, LF.1444; for a batch, the batch as a whole), and its earlier-attempt line says only that it wrote nothing, never the fixer's account of a change is "left open: nothing was changed", whatever the fixer lists. A kept fix whose own tests still fail is read for where they fail (LF.1272, `judged_fixes.progress`): the defect's tests as the stage found them (`.harness/fix-profiles.json`, kept only when the suite's saved result is of the product as it was) against the suite's result after it. One that passes now, fails at a later line of the test (the outermost frame of the test's own code, `first_failing_step.where_the_test_was`), or with an answer nearer the one wanted (a thrown error, then a 5xx, then a 4xx, then an assertion on what came back) - with none failing earlier, further from its answer, or newly failing - moved forward: the attempt is `moved forward: <tests> now fail at <step>`, the defect's actual behaviour carries that evidence for the next fixer (replacing the last such note), and it is not counted as a failed fix (as unverified is not). Where no failure can be read either side (no frames, no answers), it is "left open: its tests still fail" and counted, as before; the revert rule is not loosened by progress. The start-break note (`agents/start_breaks.py`) is ended here, only by a kept fix that changed the product and was measured with it starting.

### 7.3a What a fixer's request holds (LF.1270, LF.1274)

- **Fresh evidence, always** (`graph/fresh_evidence.py`): each time a defect is shown to a fixer its actual behaviour is
  read against the latest saved measurement of the product as it is now (its fingerprint): the server log it carries is
  replaced by the latest one (dropped when the latest logged nothing), a failing criterion's opening line of failed tests
  is the latest one, and a revert note for a start that failed is dropped once the latest measurement finds the product
  starting. A defect raised for one step many tests stop at (it names no criterion) is shown its tests as the latest
  measurement found them, not the last failing command. The stored defect is unchanged (revert tallies and disputes read it).
  The note that the server hid a 5xx's cause is dropped once the latest log states a cause (LF.1410).
  Only the failed-tests sentence is replaced: the label of the page at load ("What the product shows when it loads,
  before any test touches it:") stands on its own line and is kept, also on a defect stored with it at the end of the
  first line (LF.1458).
- **The last failing command only when it is about the defect** (`observability/transcript.last_failing_command(...,
  about=defect)`, LF.1456, always on): the fallback after a criterion's, a re-measure's and the unit report's evidence
  is shown only for a command on the run's live branch, within 60 minutes of the latest command, that exited above zero
  (a withheld repeat's -1 is not a failure), is not a stage's probe (a scratch file, or an interpreter's inline `-c`/`-e`
  script; a shell's `-c` is not one), and whose output names one of the defect's files or prints its criterion in the
  lines shown. Otherwise the section is left out.
- **Claims as claims** (LF.1462, LF.1463, always on): a code review finding's defect (`engineering.review_defect`) holds
  under "Actual behavior" only what measured it (`what_shows_it`'s sentence) and says that the reading in its
  description is the reviewer's, not measured, to check before changing anything; "Earlier attempts" labels a fixer's
  own account `it said, not measured: "..."`.
- **Its criterion run on its own** (`graph/first_failure.py`, LF.1411, always on): before a fixer is shown a failing
  criterion, the files holding that criterion's tests are run on their own as the suite runs one file
  (`per_file.each_file(..., with_tests=True)`, read from the dispatch-time re-measure of the same product when there was
  one) and the request carries the first failure with its message, the server's error from that run, and each other
  failure in a line, within 1,500 characters - where a whole-suite run differs (a limit another test used up, shared
  state), this is how the product behaves now. After each write the fixer keeps whose check still fails, the write's
  reply adds the same (`verify_after_write.watching(then=...)`): the first failure now and the server's error.
- **A server error with no logged cause comes first** (LF.1410, always on): when a failing test received a 5xx or lost
  its connection, and neither the run on its own nor the suite's last run logged an error (an access line states none),
  the request asks first for the handler to log the exception with its trace, then to run the failing test, naming the
  product's lines that answer a server error from a handler that logs nothing; said once when the defect already
  carries the raise-time note (`acceptance_nodes.the_server_hid_the_cause`). The backend builder is told the rule
  (`agents/server_errors_logged.py`).
- **The files a 5xx's logged trace names** are a failing criterion's affected files when its failed tests received a 5xx
  (LF.1564, `acceptance/logged_files.py`): each frame's path under the workspace or the sandbox's mount that is a
  product file (no test, dependency or build output), the frame that raised first (a `File "...", line N` trace is read
  from its end), so the rewrite after a surrender has the file the error came from.
- **What the server logged** is read by one reader (`what_went_wrong.the_errors_logged`, used by
  `suite.what_the_server_logged`, LF.1410): access lines are never errors; each stated error (Python, JavaScript, Java,
  .NET, PHP, Rust, Go, structured `level=error`) is kept once (numbers and times aside) with up to three frames in the
  product's own files (library frames left out); the cap (12 lines, 1,500 characters) keeps the latest, so a chain's
  root cause survives. The lines answering a 5xx are found by number or named constant (`HTTP_500_...`,
  `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR`).
- **Where the failure is handled, found first** (FX.2, LF.1416, always on, `graph/localised_fix.py`): the requests its
  failing tests made (`POST /api/x` in a log, `.post('/api/x'` in a test's code) are matched to the routes the code
  graph read when it was built (KG.4, LF.1514) - a quoted path beside a routing word, any language
  (`declares_a_route`; files the graph does not hold are still scanned) - by the end of the path, the request's own
  method first; the handler's file and the declaring file are named, and under "Where each route is answered" the
  handler by name and lines; the product's own frames in a stack trace are added; for a page, the files the graph shows
  rendering exactly the text its failing selectors name (how `page`); where none places it and the defect names no
  file, one localisation call without reasoning is asked (`localisation.asked`, the
  rewrite path's, whatever `LOCALISE_FIRST` says), once a fixer. Its map of what each file defines reads a C#, Java,
  PHP or Kotlin definition with its modifiers (`public class`, `final class`, `public function`; LF.1491). The files found and what they import (the code graph)
  are named in the evidence under "Where the failure is handled"; the memory holds them whole first, and a sliced
  fixer memory no longer holds the locked suite's files its tests' output alone named (`held_whole`); past the target,
  the memory keeps room for them, at most `MOST_SHARE_HELD` = 0.6 of the target. One `fixer_localised` record a fixer
  (how: route, trace, asked or none). What the code graph says (KG.1, LF.1509, a record, no behaviour): each fix
  request and work order's request that asks the graph (`PROJECT_GRAPH_FIXES`, `PROJECT_GRAPH_BUILDERS`) leaves one
  `project_graph_reach` record - its files, and by language how many the graph holds, their import edges both ways and
  the acceptance test files that reach them, and whether a neighbourhood was carried - once per stage, files and
  neighbourhood, not on each resume. A carried one is the two switches' firing signal (`switch_firing.SIGNALS`), and
  `loop.sh effects` reports per purpose the share of requests whose files the graph named no edge and no test for, by
  language, and the fixers localised by how. Only source files are counted by language - a file of the language table
  or one the graph holds; a `.txt` or `.json` the request names is left out (it read "txt 7 of 7", LF.1536), and a
  request naming no source file counts under "no source file".
- **A lean fixer** (FX.3, LF.1417-LF.1418, always on, `agents/lean_fixer.py`): a fixer is its own agent
  (`create_specialist_agent(fixing=True)`), not its owner's builder: a short prompt (how to find the cause, read by
  range, change by exact passage, run the test again, answer once; the owner's product rules, no logic removed, no
  route only for tests, the locked suite and `docs/` left alone; about 6,400 characters against the builder's 15,300
  before the stack's guidance) and its few tools - `read_file`, `grep`, `edit_artifact`, `write_artifact`,
  `delete_artifact` (a stack's guidance names it for a lock file), `run_terminal_command`, `read_command_output` (the
  harness's own pointers name it), `module_exports` where the run offers it - beside its answer, and the web tools
  when a twice-failed fixer is given them (16,000 characters of descriptions against 23,600). The page viewer is
  not offered, so the request's "See the page" section is left out. What its failing tests printed shows each way they
  failed once, whole, and every other test that failed the same way as one line with where it stopped
  (`failures_once`).
- **Sized to a target** (one input budget per request, `agents/request_budget.py`; the fixer's shape in
  `graph/fixer_request_shape.py`): the opening request - request and project memory - leaves room
  for `WORKING_CALLS` = 10 calls of one command's output each before the first summary (`ContextBudget.trigger_tokens`;
  at least 24,000 characters). A fixer's target also takes off its agent's system prompt and tool descriptions and is
  at most what they leave of `FIXER_OPENING_SHARE` = 0.5 of the trigger (`request_budget.fixer_target`, FX.3, LF.1419;
  the memory middleware sizes the memory with the call's exact framing). Under it the request is as built. Past it: the defect's evidence first (defect,
  reproduction, expected, actual, files, its failing tests' output, bundled failures), then an open defect the previous
  round raised for a step this defect's tests stop at, named as the shared cause; then what is kept whole (how the fix is
  settled, earlier attempts as measured, fixes to keep, the cause journal, the stub note); then, under one heading, the
  standing context cut to what the defect touches - the API only for the routes its failing tests call (whole when none
  can be read), facts and unserved-code lines only about its files and their imports - and the project memory holds whole
  only the files its evidence names and what they import (`project_memory.render_for_a_stage`). Still past the target,
  whole standing sections go, those that do not bear on the defect first (that name none of its files, the routes its
  tests call or its criteria, `lean_fixer.bearing_on`, LF.1418), largest first, each named with where to read it.
  Measured by rebuilding a medium run's AC-1 fixer request: 129,967 characters (31,776 tokens) before; 81,100 after
  (about 19,800 tokens, 0.46 of the 43,020-token trigger), the route file and the database module held whole in
  place of the locked suite's test and helper.
- **The stack block and the setup block say what the specification and the install decided** (LF.1477, LF.1481): the
  stack selector's other tools are named as chosen with the stack, "the specification decides the libraries", and a
  tool the specification replaced (a decision record saying `instead of`/`no dependency on`/`avoids`/`over` it, or one
  titled with the tool's purpose deciding without it; never one its library calls name) is named apart as not used
  (`agents/stack_reconciled.py`, read from `docs/technical_specification.md`); the library calls are "the
  specification decided", a version that disagrees with `.harness/stack-facts.json` given as installed, saying so. The
  project setup keeps its files apart from its manifest scripts: the files are not changed, tested, executed on their
  own or built on; the scripts are not changed and are how the tests run (`contract.describe`; the digest says the same
  of a kept file).
- **The rewrite path's request under the same budget** (`rewritten_fixes.fix_request`, LF.1274): one file's rewrite past
  the target opens with its instruction, the defect, what failed, the bundled failures, the compiler's word and the file
  to correct (a long file as the parts its failure points at); keeps earlier attempts, the cause journal, the fixes to
  keep and a manifest's registry releases whole; cuts the API to the routes its failing tests call (the rest named in
  `/docs/technical_specification.md`), the facts to its files (rulings and start breaks whole) and the project to the
  files its evidence names and their imports; and ends with its reply instruction. Within the target it is as built.
- **A review's memory under it, behind `OPENING_ROOM_FOR_REVIEWS`** (group Context and cost, off until measured, `docs/measuring.md` M22, LF.1472; `project_memory._a_review_within_its_room`): a stage whose request names the files its memory holds (the lead, the code review, a test round, the QA lead) is cut by the opening rule below like any other stage - its memory rendered within what the target leaves beside its request, the files under review first - and its request's line of held files names only those held. Cut so, the code under review comes first, the files the request's work bears on first among it, its configuration and tests after it, and no file outside the review is held whole (LF.1567; measured: the cut held the migration tool's environment, the manifest, the tool's settings and the requirements files, and left out the batch's page, components, hook and authentication module). Off, such a memory is the model's share whatever the request's size (measured: the lead opened at 173,770 characters, the code review at 1.16 of its trigger).
- **Every other stage's memory under it, behind `REQUEST_BUDGET_FOR_EVERY_STAGE`** (group Context and cost, off until
  measured, `docs/measuring.md` M17; `project_memory.render_for_a_stage`): with it on, a stage whose request and memory
  are past the target is given the memory within what the target leaves beside its request (at least a quarter of
  it), each long workflow document held as its outline by line and the sections its request points at, at most
  `DOCUMENTS_PAST_THE_BUDGET` = 12,000 characters (measured: a medium run's builders, lead, planner, reviews and suite
  author carried 53-69K characters of the specification's digest beside the parts their requests quoted). Small runs
  open past the target too, hence the switch. Off, or within the target, the memory is as before.
- **Always, where the opening leaves no room before the first summary** (PA.1, LF.1387, `agents/opening_room.py`): the
  project-memory middleware also counts the call's system prompt and tool descriptions (`framing_of`); where those,
  the request and the memory together reach `OPENING_SHARE` = 0.8 of the stage's summary trigger (at 3 characters a
  token), a request past the target gets the same cut whatever the switch says, and its `request_budget` record says
  so (`applied` true, its own message, not counted as the switch's firing). The memory chosen again at a summary
  (LF.1288) is held to the same room when the switch or this rule cut the opening (`_room_the_opening_leaves`);
  rendered whole there, a stage cut at its opening was back past its trigger after its first summary. Measured on a medium run's first-sprint
  builders (91,776-token slot, trigger 43,020): 21K characters of system prompt, 24K of tool descriptions and a 135K
  first message, 47,124 tokens counted by the server before any read - 1.38 of the trigger, summarised every 4-6 calls;
  on four small runs (182,272-token slot) no stage's opening passed 0.70 of its trigger, so nothing there changes.
- **A whole read past the room left, behind `READS_SIZED_TO_THE_ROOM`** (group Context and cost, off until measured,
  `docs/measuring.md` M18, LF.1289; `agents/room_for_reads.py`, `WorkspaceBackend.read`): the room a stage's next
  request has is the last reply's reported `input_tokens` against the stage's input ceiling, less what the tool results
  after that reply returned. A `read_file` naming no `offset` and no `limit` of a file longer than 80 lines whose
  numbered text is larger than that room is answered with the file's length and outline - each definition with its
  first and last line (`app.analysis.outline.entries`; the top-level definition lines without it) - and how to read the
  lines it needs by range. A range read is never changed; before the first reply the room is not known and a read is
  as before. Its answers say "more than this request has room left for", the firing signal `loop.sh effects` reads.
- **A narrow range read widened to the room, behind `READ_WINDOWS_SIZED_TO_THE_ROOM`** (group Context and cost, off in
  code and on in the compose file, `docs/measuring.md` M-LF.1570; `agents/read_windows.py`, through the same
  middleware and `WorkspaceBackend.read`): a `read_file` naming a `limit` below the 400-line default, of a file holding
  more lines past its `offset`, is given more lines from the same start - up to the default window, the file's end and
  half the room left (the same accounting), stopping before lines whose numbered text the conversation still holds
  (`settled_answer.reads_in_the_conversation`, LF.1517, and the windows it widened still in view, this reply's earlier
  ones included). One line ahead of the numbered text says "The read was widened to lines a-b of n (you asked for
  a-c)", the firing signal. The model's own arguments are not changed, so the memory's and the repeat refusals decide
  the read as before; before the first reply, or past the room, a read is as asked. Measured before it: a medium run's
  code review made 62 reads, almost all 50-60-line windows, at about 40k tokens of conversation a call. `loop.sh
  effects` (LF.1570 line) gives the reads widened and, per review and per fixer visit, the median reads and input
  tokens.
- **Recorded**: each check is one `request_budget` transcript record (stage, target, size built and sent with its
  memory, sections cut, sections left out, `applied`; with the switch off a stage's record says what would have been
  cut, `applied` false), counted by `loop.sh effects` (cut, and apart, would be cut).

### 7.4 Compiler and language-service feedback

`analysis/language_service.py` runs the TypeScript language service (JS/TS) and Pyright (Python) on every written file: names nothing defines, imports of missing exports or modules. Findings are notes at the write and defects at CI (`engineering.underlined_in_the_batch`). Pyright resolves imports from each Python package's own root: every folder up to three levels below the project holding a manifest (`pyproject.toml`, `setup.py`, `setup.cfg`, `pytest.ini`, `tox.ini`, `requirements.txt`) is an execution environment of its own, deepest first, the project last, each also reading the other roots and each root's `src` (`language_service.import_roots`, LF.1455); a type's opinion of an assignment (`... is not assignable`, `Cannot assign to attribute`) is not kept. A finding that an import names nothing which the latest measurement contradicts - tests of that file passed, or, for product code, the product started and acceptance tests passed against it - is raised as the service's word, minor, "a suspected false finding", never reverts a fix (`what_the_fix_made_certain_to_fail(..., measured)`), and is recorded in `.harness/check-findings.jsonl` under "language service, contradicted by the measurement" (`analysis/checker_contradicted.py`). So is one its fixer's own run showed false (LF.1563, `analysis/imports_a_fixer_ran.py`): after a surrender, a command of that attempt that exited 0 and imports the missing name or the finding's module means no rewrite after the surrender; the finding is recorded under "language service, shown false by its fixer's run" with the file's content digest, and while the file is unchanged the gate words it as the service's, minor. `analysis/compiler_check.py` gives compiled languages the compiler as language service (gopls, rust-analyzer, C#/Java servers) where available. `analysis/package_exports.py` (`module_exports`) tells a stage what an installed package really exports before it imports a name. For Rust, a compiler error about an import or path a crate does not have where the code asks (`unresolved import`, `could not find ... in`) carries where the crate really declares the name, or its nearest names: `analysis/rust_crate_exports.py` reads the crate's source where cargo unpacked it under the workspace's `.agent-home` (the sandbox's HOME), through `language_service`'s per-suffix table, into the compiler findings a stage is told of and the sprint gate's compile defects. `analysis/dependency_drift.py` bundles imports no manifest declares into one defect; `analysis/reachability.py` finds source files nothing imports (the product's second copy), reported at release as "code the product does not run"; `analysis/integration_checks.py` checks cross-file references written separately.

### 7.5 Known-error causes

A pack's `known_errors` and the harness's recognisers (`app/stacks/known_errors.py`, `acceptance/what_went_wrong.py`, `acceptance_nodes.where_errors_become_5xx` and `the_server_hid_the_cause`) put a stated cause on a defect: the server's real exception when it was hidden behind a generic 500, the error line rather than the end of a stack, a colour that reads for a contrast failure (`a_shade_that_reads`).

### 7.6 The journal, history and stuck lookups

- **Cause journal** (`graph/cause_journal.py`, `.harness/fix-journal.json`): what was tried on each cause across re-plans, rewinds and new fix cycles - files changed, the fixer's own one-line account, how the harness measured it. Fixers and the lead's re-plan request read it back newest first, bounded.
- **Earlier attempts** (`agents/ledger.py`, `DECISION_LEDGER`): each earlier attempt as measured, not a revert sentence; the last permitted attempt is given a different shape of work (`_the_last_attempt_section`).
- **More lookup triggers** (LF.1170): an install or setup error the registry note does not explain (T2) and a compiler error code repeated from the round before (T3) are looked up the same way, once per run per error, one block per fixer; before a dependency manifest is drafted or rewritten, the registry's current release of each dependency the specification names is shown (T4, `graph/registry_versions.py`, registries only - runs under every lookups choice).
- **Web tools after two failed fixes** (LF.1172, `WEB_TOOLS_AFTER_TWO_FAILED_FIXES`, off): once a defect's fixes have failed twice, that fixer alone gets `search_the_web` and `read_web_page`, told so in one sentence; never for a mutation finding or when the run's lookups choice is Off.
- **Lookup sources, order and caps** (LF.1173; one reader since LF.1179, each connection made to the address it checked): Stack Exchange, GitHub issues and the private search first; Context7, Brave and Tavily only when those found fewer than 2 results (Brave and Tavily only with a key). `LOOKUP_ORDER` reorders, `LOOKUP_DAILY_CAPS` sets per-source daily caps (defaults: Stack Exchange 250, GitHub 500, private search 1,000, Context7 100, Brave and Tavily 50). **Refused is not "nothing found"** (LF.1294, plan KS.3, `tools/source_breaker.py`): a 429, a 403 or 503 with `Retry-After`, GitHub's `x-ratelimit-remaining: 0` (until `x-ratelimit-reset`) and Stack Exchange's throttle are refusals; Stack Exchange's `backoff` holds the source even on an answer; a refusal with no stated time holds it for an exponential backoff with jitter (60 s doubling, at most an hour). The holds are kept in the lookups' shared store (below) for every process and machine; a held source is skipped and the answer says "refused until ...", any other error status counts as a failure, and neither is ever cached or recorded as "nothing found". Each answer carries `by_source` (results each source gave, or `refused` / `failed`). A true "nothing found" - every source asked answered, none skipped - is cached for a day (`NOTHING_FOUND_DAYS`). **One search path** (LF.1294, plan KS.4): a model's `search_the_web` asks Stack Exchange and GitHub through their APIs (counted, held when they refuse) beside the private search, whose own Stack Exchange and GitHub engines are removed from `docker/searxng/settings.yml`, as are the engines that refuse a self-hosted instance (Google, Bing, Brave, Startpage, Mojeek); DuckDuckGo and Wikipedia stay as the general pair, `suspended_times` are set explicitly, and engines the search names in `unresponsive_engines` are reported as not answering rather than "nothing was found". `tools/page_reader.py` reads one page: public addresses only, re-checked on every redirect hop, robots.txt obeyed, main text with code kept as fenced blocks (a `<pre>` becomes a fence with its class's language, inline `<code>` backquoted; an excerpt is cut at a block's end, never inside a fence - LF.1294, plan KS.2), paged; an address its site offers an official channel for is read through it and never as a page (LF.1294, plan KS.1, `tools/source_readers.py`): a Stack Exchange question by id through the Stack Exchange API (question and answers, the accepted answer first, then by votes, each with date, score and author, the link kept), a GitHub issue or pull request through the REST API with its comments (the maintainer's reply nearest its closing first - `author_association` OWNER, MEMBER or COLLABORATOR - its state and the changes the replies link), a GitHub file from the raw host, a release list through the releases API, a repository root through its README, and an npm, PyPI or crates.io package page from its registry's own document; every such request passes the outbound gate (the Stack Exchange key added after the record) and is counted against its source's daily cap (`github` 1,000, `github files` 2,000); no browser render (Playwright for Python is not in the worker image). robots.txt is read as RFC 9309 says (LF.1297, plan KS.7, `tools/robots.py`): `*` and a closing `$` in a rule's path are honoured (GitHub's `Disallow: /*/tree/` applies), the longest matching rule wins and an allow wins a tie, the reader's own group is obeyed before `*`; a site's answer is kept per host for 24 hours (a missing file allows everything; an unreachable one refuses that read and is asked again next time). **One store for every run, machine and tenant** (LF.1304, plan KS.5, the first slice of HK.12, always on; `tools/lookup_store.py`): the lookups' answers, each source's daily count, the breaker's holds and robots.txt answers read through the network's own transport are kept in the deployment's Redis (`REDIS_URL`, chosen over a Postgres table because each need is one of Redis's own atomic operations: a counted request checked against its caps in one script, a lease that is a key set only if absent and lapses by itself, entries that expire on their own; no schema). An answer is kept 7 days, a true "nothing found" 1 day, robots.txt 24 hours; an answer is shared between tenants only when its query is public - the outbound gate, given the asking run's workspace, would send it unchanged - and otherwise kept for that tenant alone (the tenant is the first folder of the run's workspace); a key is a hash and never names who asked. **Single-flight**: the first asker of a query takes a 120-second lease on it and concurrent askers of the same query wait for its answer instead of asking outward. **Counts**: one request is counted against the source's daily cap and the tenant's daily budget (`LOOKUP_TENANT_DAILY_CAP`, default 5,000 requests a day, about the sum of the sources' keyless caps) in one atomic step, so requests sent at once never pass either (measured before: 30 requests sent at once against a cap of 5 all went out). **Frozen snapshot** (`LOOKUPS_FROZEN=<label>`, for a Measure batch): the first read of a query under a label copies the answer the store holds then, or the one it asks for, into the snapshot, where it never goes stale, so both arms of a pair and a rerun read identical answers (a refusal is never kept, so it is not frozen). `LOOKUPS_STORE_PREFIX` names a deployment's keys (default `lookups`). When `REDIS_URL` is unset or the store does not answer, every operation falls back to this machine's files under `DATA_DIR/lookups` with a process lock and a file lock, and the store is tried again after 30 s; a lookup never fails a stage for it. Not yet in the store (later HK.12 slices): the registry's and the documentation reader's per-process caches, the once-per-run record of a stuck lookup (`stuck_lookup._DONE`), per-source token buckets.
- **Internet lookups, chosen per run** (`app/internet_lookups.py`): *Off* sends nothing outside except package registries and official docs; *When stuck* turns on the stuck lookup below; *Always* (the new-run form's default since 2026-11-23, and the deployed default for every run) also offers web search and the page reader to the stages that read docs (`WEB_TOOLS_OFFERED`) - or, by what the model diagnosis measured, one `research` tool in their place or no web tool (8.3, HK.11). The choice is the run's own switches and shows on the run page; the Stack Exchange key and GitHub token are set on the admin page, masked, and never reach a model's environment.
- **Lookup sources, order and caps** (LF.1173; one reader since LF.1179, each connection made to the address it checked): Stack Exchange, GitHub issues and the private search first; Context7, Brave and Tavily only when those found fewer than 2 results (Brave and Tavily only with a key). `LOOKUP_ORDER` reorders, `LOOKUP_DAILY_CAPS` sets per-source daily caps (defaults: Stack Exchange 250, GitHub 500, private search 1,000, Context7 100, Brave and Tavily 50). **Refused is not "nothing found"** (LF.1294, plan KS.3, `tools/source_breaker.py`): a 429, a 403 or 503 with `Retry-After`, GitHub's `x-ratelimit-remaining: 0` (until `x-ratelimit-reset`) and Stack Exchange's throttle are refusals; Stack Exchange's `backoff` holds the source even on an answer; a refusal with no stated time holds it for an exponential backoff with jitter (60 s doubling, at most an hour). The holds are kept in the lookups' shared store (below) for every process and machine; a held source is skipped and the answer says "refused until ...", any other error status counts as a failure, and neither is ever cached or recorded as "nothing found". Each answer carries `by_source` (results each source gave, or `refused` / `failed`). A true "nothing found" - every source asked answered, none skipped - is cached for a day (`NOTHING_FOUND_DAYS`). **One search path** (LF.1294, plan KS.4): a model's `search_the_web` asks Stack Exchange and GitHub through their APIs (counted, held when they refuse) beside the private search, whose own Stack Exchange and GitHub engines are removed from `docker/searxng/settings.yml`, as are the engines that refuse a self-hosted instance (Google, Bing, Brave, Startpage, Mojeek); DuckDuckGo and Wikipedia stay as the general pair, `suspended_times` are set explicitly, and engines the search names in `unresponsive_engines` are reported as not answering rather than "nothing was found". `tools/page_reader.py` reads one page: public addresses only, re-checked on every redirect hop, robots.txt obeyed, main text with code kept as fenced blocks (a `<pre>` becomes a fence with its class's language, inline `<code>` backquoted; an excerpt is cut at a block's end, never inside a fence - LF.1294, plan KS.2), paged; an address its site offers an official channel for is read through it and never as a page (LF.1294, plan KS.1, `tools/source_readers.py`): a Stack Exchange question by id through the Stack Exchange API (question and answers, the accepted answer first, then by votes, each with date, score and author, the link kept), a GitHub issue or pull request through the REST API with its comments (the maintainer's reply nearest its closing first - `author_association` OWNER, MEMBER or COLLABORATOR - its state and the changes the replies link), a GitHub file from the raw host, a release list through the releases API, a repository root through its README, and an npm, PyPI or crates.io package page from its registry's own document; every such request passes the outbound gate (the Stack Exchange key added after the record) and is counted against its source's daily cap (`github` 1,000, `github files` 2,000); no browser render (Playwright for Python is not in the worker image). robots.txt is read as RFC 9309 says (LF.1297, plan KS.7, `tools/robots.py`): `*` and a closing `$` in a rule's path are honoured (GitHub's `Disallow: /*/tree/` applies), the longest matching rule wins and an allow wins a tie, the reader's own group is obeyed before `*`; a site's answer is kept per host for 24 hours (a missing file allows everything; an unreachable one refuses that read and is asked again next time). **One store for every run, machine and tenant** (LF.1304, plan KS.5, the first slice of HK.12, always on; `tools/lookup_store.py`): the lookups' answers, each source's daily count, the breaker's holds and robots.txt answers read through the network's own transport are kept in the deployment's Redis (`REDIS_URL`, chosen over a Postgres table because each need is one of Redis's own atomic operations: a counted request checked against its caps in one script, a lease that is a key set only if absent and lapses by itself, entries that expire on their own; no schema). An answer is kept 7 days, a true "nothing found" 1 day, robots.txt 24 hours; an answer is shared between tenants only when its query is public - the outbound gate, given the asking run's workspace, would send it unchanged - and otherwise kept for that tenant alone (the tenant is the first folder of the run's workspace); a key is a hash and never names who asked. **Single-flight**: the first asker of a query takes a 120-second lease on it and concurrent askers of the same query wait for its answer instead of asking outward. **Counts**: one request is counted against the source's daily cap and the tenant's daily budget (`LOOKUP_TENANT_DAILY_CAP`, default 5,000 requests a day, about the sum of the sources' keyless caps) in one atomic step, so requests sent at once never pass either (measured before: 30 requests sent at once against a cap of 5 all went out). **Frozen snapshot** (`LOOKUPS_FROZEN=<label>`, for a Measure batch): the first read of a query under a label copies the answer the store holds then, or the one it asks for, into the snapshot, where it never goes stale, so both arms of a pair and a rerun read identical answers (a refusal is never kept, so it is not frozen). `LOOKUPS_STORE_PREFIX` names a deployment's keys (default `lookups`). When `REDIS_URL` is unset or the store does not answer, every operation falls back to this machine's files under `DATA_DIR/lookups` with a process lock and a file lock, and the store is tried again after 30 s; a lookup never fails a stage for it. **The rest of HK.12** (LF.1308, always on; `tools/lookup_service.py` on the same store): **rates** - each source has a token bucket shared by every run and machine, taken in one Lua step by the store's own clock (Stack Exchange 10 a second, GitHub search 10 a minute and 30 with a token, GitHub's core API 60 an hour and 5,000 with a token, GitHub's raw host 60 a minute, Context7 60 a minute, Brave and Tavily 1 a second, crates.io 1 a second for every registry read; `LOOKUP_RATES` as `name=N/s|m|h`, 0 for none); a request waits up to `LOOKUP_MOST_WAIT` (10 s) for its token, else it is not sent and counts as a failure, never "nothing found". **Registries, documentation and pages**: a registry's answer about a public package's current release (`package_registry.look_up`, keyed by this environment's runtime so a toolchain's fitted release is never given to another) and each release list (`release_fit.releases`) are kept an hour, what one release states (Go and NuGet needs) 30 days, an official documentation page (`docs`) a day and a missing one an hour, a page read through the network's own transport (`page_reader`, including the official channels) an hour or a week for a versioned address (a release tag, a commit-pinned file, a full version in the path) - shared between tenants when the address holds none of the tenant's words, else kept for that tenant; the product's own package name, a stub's answers, a run whose lookups are "off" and a value over 192 KB stay in the asking process's cache as before. **A run's own record**: the stuck lookup's once-per-run record is the run's (by run id) in the store for 7 days, so a deploy, a restart or a second worker of the run does not look the same error up again. It is also kept in the run's workspace (`.harness/lookups-this-run.json`, `tools/lookups_this_run.py`, at most 200 values, a workspace a new run reuses starting empty) and read from there first, so a store too full to take it loses nothing (LF.1505: measured, a full store sent one fixer's query ten times in eleven minutes). **"Off"**: a run whose Internet lookups choice is "off" neither reads nor writes any kept answer, document, page, robots.txt or snapshot; counts, holds and buckets still apply (they carry no query). **Paid search**: Brave and Tavily are asked only after every free source whatever `LOOKUP_ORDER` names, and an answer holding their results is never kept unless `LOOKUP_PAID_RESULTS_KEPT` names the provider (default none: Brave's terms restrict storing results). **Room for the queue**: the store is the queue's Redis (`maxmemory 512mb`, `noeviction`), so a lookup value is written there only while its memory is under `LOOKUP_STORE_MEMORY_SHARE` (default half) of `maxmemory`, else to this machine's files, and a value the store does not hold is read from those files (LF.1505: before it, nothing kept while the store was full was ever found again); every value has a lifetime, so the lookups' own share is bounded - on the measured full store they held a few kilobytes, the rest was the queue's and the events' records. `loop.sh lookups` reads every source live (nothing kept is read) and adds each source's daily allowance used, this tenant's budget, each bucket's tokens left and the store's memory with the lookups' key count and bytes; when full for lookups the line says how much is not theirs and that their values are read back from this machine's files (also in `loop.sh ready`).
- **Internet lookups, chosen per run** (`app/internet_lookups.py`): *Off* sends nothing outside except package registries and official docs; *When stuck* turns on the stuck lookup below; *Always* (the new-run form's default since 2026-11-23, and the deployed default for every run) also offers web search and the page reader to the stages that read docs (`WEB_TOOLS_OFFERED`). The choice is the run's own switches and shows on the run page; the Stack Exchange key and GitHub token are set on the admin page, masked, and never reach a model's environment.
- **Product research for the product manager** (LF.1305, plan HK.10; `PRODUCT_RESEARCH` - group "Internet lookups", off in code and in the compose file; `agents/product_research.py`): two keys - the switch, and the run owner's consent for that run (the new-run form's "Product research for this run", unticked each time and never saved with the form's defaults; `product_research` on the start request, kept as the run's own setting `PRODUCT_RESEARCH_CONSENTED`, which `switches.for_the_run` alone reads - an admin setting, an environment variable or a `switches` entry cannot give it, the last refused as unknown). Not under Internet lookups Off, nor without a search service. With both, before the intake: one planning-model call names the brief's product type in at most six plain words and lists the names the brief gives; the harness takes those names, joined identifiers (`LinkShelf`) and quoted names out through the outbound gate and sends at most three queries built from the type alone (`<type> features`, `best <type>`, `<type> alternatives`), each an `outbound` record (channel `product research`), to the private search's general engines only; one more call summarises the results (at most ten) into at most six suggestions, each citing the numbered results - one citing none is dropped. The intake request carries them under "Comparable products: suggestions the brief does not require" (the switch-firing signal) with the rule that one taken goes in deferred scope with its id. After the intake, any suggestion found elsewhere - by id, or by a feature of two or more words written out whole that the brief does not name - is moved to deferred scope, marked "a suggestion from comparable products; the brief does not require it"; one the brief names stays, marked as also common. Acceptance criteria come from the brief, so none becomes one. Recorded: `.harness/research/product-research.json` (type, queries, sources, suggestions, taken and where, moved), one decision (point `product_research`) and `product_research` transcript records. Done once a run: a re-run intake is shown the same note. With either key off, nothing is read, sent or written and the intake is byte for byte as before. Measured by owner review, not hidden tests (`docs/measuring.md` M-HK.10).
- **One outbound gate** (LF.1291, plan HK.5, always on; `tools/outbound.py`): every query that leaves the machine - a stuck lookup's query, a package name sent to its registry, a model's own `search_the_web` query and the query string of a `read_web_page` address - passes one function. It removes paths, hosts and ports, long tokens, addresses and long quoted values, and the product's private words: the workspace folder, the contract's project, package and title, the product's name from the intake, and the nouns its routes are named by (read from the specification and the locked contract; route words every web product has, such as `api`, `auth` and `users`, are left in). Each word is removed in every spelling and in its plural, also inside a joined identifier (`LinkShelf`, `reading_lists`). Public identifiers stay: error codes, versions, and the packages the project declares (`.harness/dependency-gate.json`), even when one is spelled like a route noun. A registry is never asked for the product's own name. Each query sent is one `outbound` record on the run's transcript (where it went, what was sent, how many private words were removed, never which ones); `loop.sh effects` counts them by channel. The run page does not list lookups, so it has no line for this. A caller that knows the workspace passes it; otherwise the gate finds the running run's workspace from the graph's config. Outside a run, or in a worker thread without the run's context, only the generic cleaning applies.
- **Stuck lookups** (`WEB_LOOKUP_WHEN_STUCK` - group "Internet lookups", on by default since 2026-11-23 and set per run; `graph/stuck_lookup.py`, `app/tools/lookups.py`): when a defect is shown to a fixer again after an attempt that did not close it and its text states a concrete error, the harness (not the model) looks the error up once per run, the way a person does (LF.1295, plan HK.3, `graph/stuck_research.py`): (a) an error saying a package has no such name - Python's `cannot import name` / `has no attribute`, TypeScript's `has no exported member`, Node's `does not provide an export named` or a subpath not in `exports`, rustc's `unresolved import`, `could not find`, `cannot find ... in crate` and a crate's type without a method or variant (LF.1490) - is answered from the installed package's own files (`analysis/installed_packages.py`, reusing `package_exports` and `rust_crate_exports`) with the lines of its shipped changelog naming the symbol, and nothing is asked of the web; (b) otherwise several short queries are tried in turn until one finds something: the error's code with the library, the message's stable words (long quoted values, long numbers, addresses and paths out), the library with its release line and the symptom; (c) the library is the first of the project's own packages the error writes (the dependency gate's record and the stack facts; a Rust path's crate), its version the one the stack facts say it resolved, else the installed files', and its GitHub repository from the facts or its registry's homes: issue search is kept to that repository, by best match with closed issues first, and asked before Stack Exchange unless the facts date the release more than two years back, giving at most 2 of the 3 leads so Stack Exchange still answers; across all of GitHub the message's own words are asked as one quoted phrase (`lookups.phrase_of`); (d) the first two issue leads are read through GitHub's API and shown with their resolution, not the reporter's question; (e) leads are ranked: the library's own repository or a maintainer, then a match with the release line the project runs, then the last two years, then votes; (f) behind `STUCK_LOOKUP_CONDENSED` (group "Internet lookups", off until measured) one call of the fixer's model condenses the leads into cause, change, versions and source, "does not apply" allowed, and the raw leads no longer enter the request; (g) with it, the harness compares the versions the note names with the release line the project runs and labels a note about another line; (h) see "nothing found" above. Each note is a line of `.harness/research/notes.jsonl` (id, defect id, trigger, queries, library, version, repository, sources, verdict - local, leads, applies, does not apply, nothing found, refused) and its ledger record carries the queries, sources and verdict too. A run with no stuck defect is unchanged. Only the cleaned error line and the stack's names leave the machine, through the one outbound gate below; results are defanged and labelled as leads (`tools/untrusted_text.py`), cached 7 days (`KEPT_DAYS`) in the shared store (LF.1304), and each lookup is a ledger record. **Lookup outcomes** (LF.1296, plan HK.4, `observability/lookup_notes.py`): each note shown has an id (from its query) on its ledger record (`lookup`, or `lookup shown again` when a note found earlier in the run is shown to another defect or round), with the trigger, the sources asked and the results each gave; the defect's next outcome is followed by a `lookup outcome` record saying whether that fix closed it. `loop.sh effects` prints the share of looked-up fixes that closed their defect beside stuck fixes made without a note (read from the ledger's order, so older runs are scored too), and each source's answer rate. **Why a fixer's lookup did not run** (LF.1425, FX.11, always on): every fixer shown leaves one `lookup gate` ledger record (once per fixer, not on each resume of its stage after a gated tool call, which is shown the same note; LF.1506) - `looked up` with its trigger, or the gate that stopped it (switched off, not stuck, no error line, mutation finding, product's own name, nothing found, refused) - and `loop.sh effects` prints the counts. **At first sight** (LF.1426, FX.9, `LOOKUP_AT_FIRST_SIGHT`, group "Internet lookups", off until measured): trigger T4 looks up, for a defect's first fixer, an error whose line writes one of the project's packages or whose trace frame nearest it lies in an installed package's files (`stuck_research.installed_package_named`), with that package as the lookup's library; once per distinct error per run, through the outbound gate, only where stuck lookups are on. **Usage notes from the installed package** (LF.1427, FX.10, `USAGE_NOTES_FROM_THE_INSTALLED_PACKAGE`, same group, off until measured, `analysis/installed_usage.py`): an edit the language service refuses, and a fixer whose defect locates lines (trace frames, `file:line`) or names a function its files call, are shown how the installed release defines those names - signature and docstring read statically from Python site-packages or JavaScript/TypeScript type files, or the nearest names for one it lacks - and the fixer one documentation example (Context7, through the outbound gate, once per name a run, when stuck lookups are on). The lookup tools stay offered to fixers: counted over 7 days, fixers called `look_up_package`/`read_docs` 19 times in 7,530 offers (LF.1428). **Replay set**: `benchmarks/lookup-replay.json` holds 25 past stuck defects with the fix that closed them (Rust, C#, Java, Python, PHP, TypeScript, Go, Ruby; data, no code), and `scripts/loop/replay_lookups.py` scores the lookup as it stands on them with no model: does the note a fixer would be shown (through `stuck_lookup.section`, or `--through lookup`) name the known fix; target 60% of the public cases. Each case carries the workspace its project had (LF.1309: the dependency gate's record and the stack facts for the packages it declared, at the releases it ran, and, where its error says a package lacks a name, that package's installed files cut to the declarations the answer reads), laid out under an empty workspace before the fixer's path asks, so the local-truth step and the library and release line fire as on a run; `--empty-workspace` asks the old way. Baseline 2026-11-25, keyless and without the private search, `--pace 7`: 4 of 23 in the case's workspace (two answered from the installed crate), 1 of 23 in an empty one the same day. Excluded: mutation findings. Optional keys raise limits (`STACKEXCHANGE_KEY`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`). The switch is off in code; the compose file and each run's own setting decide. It is read through `is_on` but is not listed in `observability/provenance.SWITCHES` or the admin groups as of this reading (unverified whether a later change adds it).
- **Research shares**: a stage visit may make at most 8 documentation reads, 12 registry lookups and 8 web lookups (`tools/research.py`); past it the stage is told to write from what it has; what was found is carried to a fresh attempt (`agents/carry_over.py`).

### 7.7 Steering and dynamic instruction injection

Steering and plugin instructions are one mechanism (3.5). `GET|POST /runs/{id}/steering` (Part 2) writes notes to `.harness/steering.json`; the instruction middleware adds the stack's guidance and the run's notes to every model call from the next call on; nothing is interrupted. A build stage, draft or rewrite that drops a declaration the pack holds (for example the build's jar name) is told the file and the exact line to restore before it returns (`entry_exports.what_a_stage_broke`, fed through the same post-write diagnostics as compiler findings). A note's first delivery marks it read by that stage (stream event and `steering_applied` record); a withdrawn note reaches no call. `where_things_stand` (`PH.1`) can add where the sprint stands to a fix request and `project_graph.with_the_neighbourhood` a defect's files' one-hop neighbours.

---

## 8. Tools

### 8.1 Workspace readers and editors (`app/tools/agent_toolkit.py`, `app/tools/repository.py`)

| Tool | Behaviour and guards |
|---|---|
| `ls`, `read_file`, `glob`, `grep` | from the deepagents filesystem backend, wrapped (`workspace_backend.py`) so `.agent-home` and installed-package folders (`vendor`, `node_modules`, `.venv`, `site-packages`) stay out of listings and searches (language-table `installed`). A file under `MOST_LINES_READ_WHOLE` = 400 lines is read whole, a longer one by windows with a note. A read of a version already seen returns the same bytes: after the first repeat the stage is told, then the read tool is withheld (refused when `STABLE_TOOLS`); project memory answers a read of a held file with a pointer; the repeated-query guard (`agents/repeated_queries.py`) covers listings and searches. The four describe themselves in the harness's words, not the library's (`agents/reader_descriptions.py`, TR.4, LF.1520): `read_file` states its real default window of 400 lines (its schema's `limit` default too), `grep` that it answers with numbered lines (`output_mode` `content` by default), and none names a tool no stage is offered (`edit_file`, `execute`, `rg`). `grep` matches plain text first, each side of a `|` alone next, and, when nothing matched and the pattern has a regular expression's characters (`^`, `$`, `.`, `*`, a class, a group), the pattern as a regular expression over the same files (package caches left out), the result saying which way it matched; an invalid expression stays a plain-text search and is said so (`WorkspaceBackend._as_an_expression`, said through the path middleware's `searched_note`, TR.5, LF.1521). |
| `ls`, `read_file`, `glob`, `grep` | from the deepagents filesystem backend, wrapped (`workspace_backend.py`) so `.agent-home` and installed-package folders (`vendor`, `node_modules`, `.venv`, `site-packages`) stay out of listings and searches (language-table `installed`). A file under `MOST_LINES_READ_WHOLE` = 400 lines is read whole, a longer one by windows with a note. A read of a version already seen returns the same bytes: after the first repeat the stage is told, then its reads are decided file by file (LF.1517), a command that only reads a file included (8.2, LF.1525); project memory answers a read of a held file with a pointer; the repeated-query guard (`agents/repeated_queries.py`) covers listings and searches. |
| `write_artifact`, `edit_artifact`, `replace_lines`, `delete_artifact`, `move_artifact` | the single-writer guards of 3.4; a file its language's parser rejects is refused with the parser's location; a stale edit (the file changed after the read) says what changed (`tools/seen_content.py`); a reply's edits to one file that parse only together are made as one edit (`agents/edits_together.py`); one `edit_artifact` call may carry up to 12 `changes` (`find`/`replace` pairs) to one file, each matched in the text the earlier ones left with a single edit's forgiveness and still once, written and checked once as a whole, a pair that matches nowhere refusing the call by its number with nothing written, and an edit refused for a name the file does not define is told to send the import and the change together that way (`repository.edit_approved_artifact_in_several_places`, LF.1423); a `find` matched ignoring its indentation has its `replace` moved to the file's margin by the same number of columns when every line is off by that one amount, spaces against spaces (`tools/edit_indentation.py`, LF.1422); an edit batch is grouped per path (`agents/line_batches.py`); an edit or write refusal names `replace_lines` as a way out only when the stage is offered it (`line_edits_offered()`): with it off, an ambiguous `find` is told to add a line that differs between the copies, and a fragment or a rewrite dropping names is sent to `edit_artifact` (LF.1516); `docs/` is the workflow's. Gated by approval when `auto_approve` is off. |
| `changed_since_sprint` | the diff against an accepted sprint's commit |
| `read_command_output` | a long command output kept out of context by reference (`tools/journal.py`, under `DATA_DIR`, not the workspace); a lost reference is answered with this run's own entries; a reference missed twice is refused |

### 8.2 Terminal and validation (`run_terminal_command`, `run_validation`; `app/tools/sandbox.py`)

- A command piped into a filter, `a | b` (a `|` item, or outside quotes in a whole line sent as one string), runs `a` under every usual check, and each command after a `|` is checked as itself - the role may run it, and it is `head`, `tail`, `grep`, `wc`, `sort`, `uniq` or `cat` with the options they are used with and no file of its own - then applied by the harness to what `a` printed (`tools/piped_commands.py`, PA.6 rest, LF.1562); `2>&1` before the `|` sends the errors through it, `2>/dev/null` drops them, and the exit code is `a`'s. A plan's command piped so is not sent back for its `|` (`planned_commands`). Any other `|` is refused as before.
- Commands are **argv lists, never strings**; no shell, so `&&`, `;`, `$(...)` reach the program as words and `cd` is not a program (`working_directory` is a parameter); a leading `cd <folder> &&` is taken as the working directory, the sandbox's `/workspace/` mount taken off its folder as off a `working_directory`. A path that repeats the working directory is shortened when the shorter one is there, a link counting as there (a virtualenv's `python` links to the sandbox image's interpreter, which the worker lacks); a failed command whose output names an argument with a `*` as written is told there is no shell to expand it, with a `find -name` argv that does (`agent_toolkit.a_star_taken_as_written`); `python -m` runs a module of the language table's `checking_modules` (`py_compile`) as it runs its test runners (TR.9, LF.1522).
- A command that only reads one workspace file is a read, decided as `read_file` is on the same call (`agents/shell_reads.py`, LF.1525): `cat`, `head`, `tail`, `sed -n` with a line range, `awk` with an `NR` range, `less`, `more`, `nl`, `wc`, and inline code that only opens and prints a file or a slice of its lines (the language table's `file_prints`: Python's `with open(...) as f: ...print(''.join(lines[a:b]))` and like forms, JavaScript's `console.log(fs.readFileSync(...))`); `a_shell_read` is the one place that says so and which lines. Where the stage's reads are decided file by file (TR.2, thinking off LF.1527), a file the memory holds whole or the request quotes whole is refused by name (`project_memory.held_read_refusal`), lines still in the conversation are refused (`settled_answer.reads_in_the_conversation` counts a command's numbered answer or its own clean, uncut output as a read), and any other file is answered from disk as `read_file` answers it - its 400-line window, numbered the same - each recorded as a `read_while_withheld` with `tool` = `run_terminal_command`; where every read is withheld whole, it is refused as `read_file` is. With reading offered, the same read sent again while its first answer is in the conversation is answered from the first. A refused editor used only to print (`sed -n 200,400p f`) is told it only reads and the `read_file` `offset`/`limit` that gives those lines, not "use `edit_artifact`"; the QA lead's command reads of the product's code are refused as its `read_file` is (LF.1531).
- The executable must be in the role's allowlist (`ROLE_EXECUTABLES`) united with the toolchain of every language the stack names (`TOOLCHAINS`, pinned by `pin_project_stack`). Argument forms that turn an allowed program back into an arbitrary one (`npm exec`, ...) are refused with the advice to save a file instead; inline code for an interpreter (`python -c`, `node -e`) is saved by the command tool to a `tmp-inline-*` file in the project and run from there, which the stack note says in those words (`specialists._no_code_on_the_command_line`, LF.1480). Which programs are there is said from one source: in container mode with a pack image, that image is asked once per run by a throwaway container running `which` over every program the harness names (`tools/image_programs.py`, `.harness/image-programs.json`; a program in the project's virtualenv counts as there), and the environment contract's "Available commands" (with the allowed ones the image lacks apart), the stack note's "not installed here" and a refusal's "Allowed and installed" all read it; without a pack image, in-container, or with execution off, the worker is asked as before (LF.1479). Servers are refused with advice (the acceptance config starts the product). Variable assignments on the command line are refused with an instruction.
- Containment: no network except the `install` category; bounded memory, CPU, processes, file size; minimal environment; a free `ACCEPTANCE_PORT` per command; timeouts per category (`install` 300 s, `build` 180, `test` 300, `default` 120, `probe` 60, `start` 20, `dry-run` 30) scaled per toolchain (`TOOLCHAIN_TIMEOUT_SCALE`: Rust 4.0, Java 3.0, ...) up to 900 s; a command is refused before it starts when the container lacks memory (`resources.py`), which converts an OOM into a result the model can read. Output is captured as head and tail with a digest, never whole (`bounded_output.py`); a test summary line ends a command early.
- **Test totals** (`_the_counts_a_test_run_reported`, LF.1485-1488): every test command's ledger entry carries the passed/failed/tests its runners printed - TAP, jest, Vitest, pytest, Playwright, cargo, go, nextest, Maven, .NET, RSpec, PHPUnit, gotestsum, Minitest, Gradle (`RUNNERS_COUNTED`) - read from the whole output before it is cut for the model, summed over every runner one command ran (a fullstack `npm test` is Vitest then pytest), each runner's last summary line; pytest's errors count as failures (not errors that stopped collection), cargo's lines are summed per test binary, nextest's summary wins over the libtest lines it prints inside failures. They feed "N of M passed", the same-result and regression notes, the suite-shrunk warning and the sprint summary's counts. Parser tests read real runner output kept in `tests/captured/`.
- Modes: `container` (a container per command, fails closed with no runtime) or `in-container`. Execution is opt-in (`ALLOW_COMMAND_EXECUTION`). Part 2 covers deployment and security.
- A command that only looks at files while the looking tools are withheld is refused (`_a_look_with_the_looking_tools_withheld`); a harness-run command is never withheld by agent repeat guards.
- Installs: the dependency gate and egress check (5.7). A runtime the image lacks is installed per project by `use_runtime` from the runtime's own distribution, checksum-verified, into a shared cache put first on that project's PATH only (`tools/runtimes.py`).

### 8.3 Package registry, docs, web (`look_up_package`, `read_docs`, `search_the_web`, `read_web_page`)
